15. cilappatikāramum maṇimēkalaiyum potuvakai kuṭi makkaḷaik ilakkiyat talaiviyarākak koṇṭavai; taṉit taṉmai vāyntavai. ivviru kāviyaṅkaḷiṉ kālam ki.pi. 350 - 450 ākalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1995). kāñcipurattil ki.pi. 5 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil vāḻnta tikñāṉar camaṟkirutattil niyāyappiravēca eṉṉum aḷavai nūlai eḻutiṉār. annūlukkum maṇimēkalaiyiṉ cila pakutikaḷukkum neruṅkiya oppumai iruppatāl maṇimēkalaiyiṉ kālattai kipi 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ taḷḷukiṟār vaiyāpuri. āṉāl maṇimēkalaiyai āṅkilattil peyartta ālaṉ tāṉiyalō (Alain Danielou) ikkaruttai maṟuttut tamiḻar aḷavai nūṟkaruttukkaḷaiyē maṇimēkalai āciriyarum, tikñāṉarum payaṉpaṭuttiyirukkalām eṉṟum maṇimēkalai kipi. iraṇṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉatāka irukkalām eṉṟum karutukiṟār.
16. mēlē pattikaḷ 7, 8l kuṟitta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tol kāppiyam kaḻaka ilakkiyac cevviyal taṉmaic ciṟappup paṟṟiya āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu; piṟa moḻiyāḷarukku ikkaruttuk kaḷai āṅkilattil terivikka ēntāka īṇṭu orē iṭattil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa :
As a source book not only of grammatical and literary study but also of human goography, social anthropology, culture, ecology and psychology, Tholkappiam contains very valuable data, and its importance for the study of Tamil culture, and of cultures in general, can hardly be exaggerated. However one must beware of taking the literary conventions as a kind of direct report, or even worse, as an objective explicit and ‘scientific treatment of 'real life'. In some works it was assumed that the bardic court-poetry reflected directly and with utmost fidelity and realism the conduct of men and women of that age in love and war. But the poetry, based on conventions, reflects “real life' only obliquely, since a literary convention was a pattern of fictional (or 'ideal') behaviour. The poets and the theoreticians had a clear understanding of the relationship between “Real life and its reflection in literature, since they clearly differentiated between the two: ulakiyal valakku denoted things said and done in ‘real life’: Ceyyul valakku (or pulaneri valakku) denoted the practice in poetry of reflecting it. A literary convention is an agreement between writer and reader that certain themes will be represented in a certain way. The reader is thus truly able to appreciate the poet's skill in handling a familiar theme by means of improvisation. The poetry of the 'interior' the subjective akam poetry is totally anonymous in the sense that akam poems never mention the hero or heroine by name, not in a single stanza. Hence they“embody what is typically human rather than what is merely individual and particular W.H.Hudson. An introduction to the study of literature, London. 1946.p97). The objective external Puram poetry is partly impersonal. partly personal. Thus, e.g. the elegies are highly personal tributes to dead patrons and friends, genuine and spontaneous, expressing intimate and personal grief, “free from any conventional bucolic machinery”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1974) Tamil Literature (in series: A History of Indian literature) Otto Harrassowitz. Wiesbaden. pp 35-36
“The discovery of this body of roughly 2300 verses (of Ettuthogai and Pathuppattu, during 1880-1910) raised the Tamil language at one stroke, from an insignificant neo-Indian Language to the standing of one of the great classical languages of the world. Whoever has made a more intimate acquaintance with this ancient poetry, will readily subscribe to the assessment of the French Indologist Pierre Meille, when he states that this poetry is comparable with the choicest gems of old Greek lytical poetry, and that this “cycle of Sangam poetry' represents one of the summits of literary creation in India and the whole world'
- K.V.Zvelebil (1960) New Orient (bi-monthly) 5, 1960pp 3. reprinted in Tamil Culture x-2: April-June 1963.
“The Dravidians, and in particular, the Tamils have contributed a great deal to the cultural richness of the world: Pallava and Chola temple architecture, Chola bronze sculpture, the dance form known as Bharatanatyam the so called Carnatic system of music. But proba bly the most significant contribution is that of Tamil literature. which still remains to be “discovered” and enjoyed by the non-Tamilians and adopted an an essential and remarkable part of universal heritage”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan--on Tamil literature of South India; Leiden: E.J.Brill. Preface
“The early Tamil poetry was rather unique not only by virtue of the fact that some of its features were so unlike everything else in India, but by virtue of its literary excellence, those 26350 lines of poetry promote Tamil to the rank of one of the great classical languages of the world--though the world at large only just about begins to realise it.
“it is only the Tamil culture that has produced - uniquely so in India, an independent indigenous literary theory of a very high standard, including metrics and prosody, poetics and rhetoric”
“there exist in India only two great specific and independent classical and historically attested cultures--the Sanskrit culture and the Tamil culture.”
“Tamil literature is the only Indian literature which is both classical and modern; while it shares antiquity with much of Sanskrit literature, and is as classical, in the best sense of the word as for example the ancient Greek poetry, it continues to be vigorously living modern writing of our days.
"Classical Tamil literature is literature about and of people. but not a Volksliterature"
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan; pp 1, 4
"Life is condidered pleasent and joyful, death an inevitable end. There is no attempt to obtain release from life. Liberality and goodness are indulged in without motives of penance or recompense. About old age thereis a note of nostalgic resignation.
“Life after death is represented as an abode of permanent happiness for the brave and good. and permanent suffering for the wicked. The happiness of future life is a reward of those who by their bravery and altruism established their glory (pukal) in this world. Love and courtship, marriage and children are considered necessary modes of personal perfection. If at all anything remains after death, it is the glory and honour, the praise due the memory of the days passed in heroism and in the service of fellow men.”
- K.V.Z. (1974) Tamil literature otto Harrassovitz, p.44.
“Ancient Tamil poetry is multivalent: it is so rich and comprehensive that it includes aesthetic structures and values which give high satisfaction to later ages and periods. It is conceived so that rather a community than a single individual could and can realize all its strata and systems. That is why it has survived generational tastes, and has acquired a permanent and universal position"
- K.V.Z (1974) ibid pp 35-36
In the depth and width of erotic experience and in the conceptual framework of the entire phenomenal world, perceived as akam and puram, Tamil culture is unique andoriginal. And as. for the parallels between Tamil and Sanskrit literature-why must it always be presumed that it was the Tamil works that borrowed from Aryan?”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1975) Tamil Literature (annals of Tamil literature, abstaining from value judgements) Leiden: E.J.Brill. p107
The ideal modern poem is expected to be objective, precise, organically complex and as well written as prose. Free from cliches an straddled adjectives, it should have the prose virtues of simplicity and hardness in a language charged with meaning to the utmost possible degree. XXxx A poem of indirection, yielding more than one layer of meaning, is a product of excellent craftsmanship. Tolka-- ppiyar's insistence on the three aspects nokku, payan and eccam, is clearly indicative of his predilection for a poetry that works subtly, indirectly and sugge stively. Nokku demands every syllable, every word and every line to be in harmony with the poem as a whole. By payan is meant the implicit statement on the purpose of an object. Though eccam which is pf two types--colleccam and kurippeccam - interpreted in diverse ways by the commentators, it is used to avoid explicit complete statements and to exploit the suggestive potential of the minimum number of words employed in the poem.
The concept of poetry as expressed in Tolhappiyar's Porulathikaram and the practice of Sangam poets as evidenced in the still cherished anthologies would reveal that the ancient Tamils had a poetics of the lyric which Elder Olson was looking for but could not find in the well known languages of the world. (Elder Olson:1962 “Sailing to Byzantium; Prolegomena to a poetics of the lyric” in Willbur Scot (ed) FIVE APPROACHES OF LITERARY CRITICISM; London-Macmillan.)
- P. Marudanayagam; CHEMMOZHI I - 2 Jan-Mar 2007
“First, Tamil is of considerable antiquity. It predates the literature of other modern Indian languages by more than a thousand years. Its oldest work, the Tolkappiyam, contains parts that, judging from the earliest Tamil inscriptions, date back to about 200 BCE. The greatest works of ancient Tamil, the Sangam anthologies and the Pattupattu, date to the first two centuries of the current era. They are the first great secular body of poetry written in India, predating Kalidasa's works by two hundred years.
“Second, Tamil constitutes the only literary tradition indigenous to India that is not derived from Sanskrit. Indeed, its literature arose before the influence of Sanskrit in the South became strong and so is qualitatively different from anything we have in Sanskrit or other Indian languages. It has its own poetic theory, its own grammatical tradition, its own esthetics, and, above all, a large body of literature that is quite unique. It shows a sort of Indian sensibility that is quite different from anything in Sanskrit or other Indian languages, and it contains its own extremely rich and intellectual tradition.
“Third, the quality of classical Tamil literature is such that it is fit to stand beside the great literatures of Sanskrit, Greek, Latin, Chinese, Persian and Arabic. The subtlety and profundity of its works, their varied scope (Tamil is the only premodern Indian literature to treat the subaltern extensively), and their universality qualify Tamil to stand as one of the great classical traditions and literatures of the world. Everyone knows the Tirukkural, one of the world's greatest works on ethics; but this is merely one of a myriad of major and extremely varied works that comprise the Tamil classical tradition. There is not a facet of human existence that is not explored and illuminated by this great literature.
“Finally, Tamil is one of the primary independent sources of modern Indian culture and tradition. I have written extensively on the influence of a Southern tradition on the Sanskrit poetic tradition. But equally important, the great sacred works of Tamil Hinduism, beginning with the Sangam Anthologies, have undergirded the development of modern Hinduis. Their ideas were taken into the Bhagavata Purana and other texts (in Telugu and Kannada as well as Sanskrit), whence they spread all over India. Tamil has its own works that are considered to be as sacred as the Vedas and that are recited alongside Vedic mantras in the great Vaishnava temples of South India (such as Tirupati). And just as Sanskrit is the source of the modern IndoAryan languages, classical Tamil is the source language of modern Tamil and Malayalam.
“The status of Tamil as one of the great classical languages of the world is something that is patently obvious to any one who knows the subject" - George L. Hart (quoted by V.C. Kulandaiswamy 2005)

iyal 4
cemmoḻit tamiḻ nūlkaḷaip
piṟamoḻikaḷil peyarttal
1. nam maṉattiluḷḷa eṇṇattaip piṟarukkut terivip pataṟkup pēcukiṟōm (orē moḻiyil pēciṉālum yāriṭam pēcukiṟōmō (naṇpar kuḻantai tāttā - pāṭṭi āciriyar mutalāḷi toḻilāḷi) ataṟkut takkavāṟu pēccu naṭai amaikiṟatu. ituvē oru vakaiyil moḻi peyarpputtāṉ eṉpār ākṭēviyō pās (Octovio Paz: "actual act of speech is a translation of thought') āyiṉum ivviyalil karutappōvatu oru moḻi nūlkaḷaip piṟamoḻikku iṉṉārukku eṉṟu kuṟippāka illāmal aṉaivarukkumāka peyarppatu paṟṟiyatu.
2. ilakkiyamallāta piṟa nūlkaḷai enta moḻiyil liruntum enta moḻikkum peyarppatil perum iṭarppāṭu illai. irumoḻikaḷilum pulamaiyum nūl nutaliya poruḷ kuṟitta aṟivum iruntāl pōtum. āyiṉum ivaṟṟil kūṭa nām potuvākak kāṇum moḻi peyarppukaḷ taramāṉavai yāka iruppatillai. itaṟkuk kāraṇam kācai maṭṭum kuṟikkōḷākak koḷvatē eṉpār cema reṉyi (Szemerenyi: “unable to understand the text, but nevertheless translate for filthy lucre'))
3. ilakkiyaṅkaḷaip piṟamoḻikaḷil peyarppatu cikkalāṉa paṇi:
(1) orē moḻik kuṭumpattaic cārnta moḻikaḷiṭaiyē ippaṇi caṟṟu eḷitu. (e.kā: tamiḻ - malaiyāḷam, tamiḻ > kaṉṉaṭam teluṅku) 
(ii) piṟamoḻik kuṭumpattaic cārntatāyiṉum moḻi yiyal taṉmaikaḷil potumaik kūṟukaḷ koṇṭa pakuti nāṭu (Linguistic area); nākarika paṇpāṭṭu orumaippāṭu koṇṭa pakuti / nāṭu eṉṟu varum poḻutu moḻipeyarppu avvaḷavu kaṭiṉamalla (e.kā: tamiḻ -> inti marātti vaṅkāḷi) 
(iii) ēṉaiya vakaip piṟamoḻikaḷil ilakkiya moḻi peyarppuk kaṭiṉamāṉatutāṉ; ataṟkut taṉittiṟamai vēṇṭum. 
atuvum kaḻaka ilakkiyam pōṉṟa 2000 āṇṭut toṉmai vāynta cevviya ilakkiyattaip piṟa moḻiyil peyarppatu mēṟkaṇṭa mūṉṟu nilaikaḷilumē kaṭumaiyāṉatu tāṉ. tamiḻ - āṅkila moḻi peyarppup paṟṟiya eṭuttuk kāṭṭukaḷ maṭṭumē ivviyalil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. āyiṉum tamiḻilakkiyattai āṅkilamallāta piṟa moḻikaḷil peyarp pataṟkum aṭippaṭaik koḷkaikaḷ poruntuvaṉavēyām.
4. ciṟanta putiṉam eḻutak kaiyēṭu oṉṟum illai . atupōlac ciṟanta ilakkiya moḻipeyarppukkum kaiyēṭu illai. eṉiṉum ilakkiya nūlkaḷai, kuṟippākac ceyyuḷ nūlkaḷai moḻipeyarkkum poḻutu piṉvarum aṟiñar kūṟṟukkaḷ karuttiṟ koḷḷat takkaṉa :
''mūlattil uḷḷatai varikku vari appaṭiyē moḻi māṟṟa vēṇṭiyatillai. mūlattiṉ pāviya uṇarvu, uvamaikaḷ - uruvakaṅkaḷ, pōṉṟavaṟṟiṉ, uyirōṭṭam moḻipeyarppil varavēṇṭum; ciṉṉañciṟu ceytikaḷai viṭṭu viṭṭālum kūṭa ''
- cuvelapil (1956): "caṅka ilakkiyap pāṭalkaḷai moḻi peyarttal - cila yōcaṉaikaḷ" (ār. kirim keyt (1971) m collukkuc col peyarppatu ciṟanta moḻi peyarppait tarātu eṉpatai viḷakkiyuḷḷār)
"moḻiyaṟivu vēṟu, ilakkiya uṇarvuvēṟu. ilakkiya uṇarvu iruntāl tāṉ uyiruḷḷa moḻipeyarppu varum. piṟamoḻi nūlaic ciṟanta moḻi peyarppiṉ (vaḻi) mutalil paṭikkum māṇavaṉ, aṭuttāṟ pōl ammoḻiyaiyum kaṟka viḻaiyak kūṭum. (māṟāka oru moḻiyaik kaṟkat toṭaṅkum māṇavaṉ ammoḻi ilakkiyattaiyum paṭikkum aḷavukkut taṉ muyaṟciyait toṭarum vāyppu kuṟaivu) ... ciṟanta eḻuttāḷaṉē ciṟanta moḻipeyarppāḷaṉ āka muṭiyum.
- kirikari rapacā (prāli : 1984 nūlil)
"moḻipeyarppu aṉaittumē mūlattaip pōṉṟa ''māyavaṭivai" (illusion) uruvākkum muyaṟciyē. cila cūḻnilai kaḷil - moḻipeyarttōṉ tiṟamaiyāl - mūlattaip pōṉṟē māyavaṭivum amaintuviṭa vāyppu uṇṭu. itu eṅku iyalum? mūlamoḻip pāviṉ vaṭivam karuttu ākiyavaṟṟukku ōraḷavu oṟṟumai uḷḷa vaṭivamum karuttum āṅkilattil irukkum nērvukaḷil tāṉ itu iyalum; attōṭu moḻipeyarttu eḻutiyatu eṉṟu koñcamum teriyāta aḷavukkuc ceyyuḷ amaiya vēṇṭum.
- hikām (1938) 
''oru moḻiyil eḻutiya kavitai iṉṉoru moḻiyil moḻi peyarkkak kūṭiyatu aṉṟu. pāvalarkaḷiṉ paṭaippu kaḷāltāṉ enta moḻiyum aḻiyāmal nilaikkiṟatu. oru moḻiyil uḷḷa aṉaittaiyum atil uḷḷavāṟē moḻipeyarppilum koṇaramuṭintuviṭṭāl am moḻiyai varuntik kaṟka yār muyalvār? oru moḻikkē iyaṟkaiyāṉa pā nayattai appaṭiyē vēṟu moḻiyil koṇara iyalātu eṉṟa nilai iruppataṉāl tāṉ am moḻik kavitaiyaip paṭittu aṉupavikka ammoḻiyaiyē nām kaṟka viḻaikiṟōm.''
- ṭākṭar jāṉcaṉ 
"kirēkka ilakkiyattai moḻipeyarppu vaḻip paṭittup payaṉ illai; mūlattai ōraḷavu aṭaiyāḷam kāṭṭavē moḻi peyarppāl iyalum.''
- varjiṉiyā ulp 
''kalviyiṉ orē nōkkam eṉṉa? hōmar kāviyaṅkaḷai mūla kirēkkattil paṭikka oruvaṉai āḷākkuvatē."
- ṣeppārṭu (1949) 
"hōmar kāviyattai "kirēkka vācakattukku iṇaiyāṉa āṅkila vācakam" eṉṟavāṟu āṅkilappaṭuttiṉāl kāviyak karuttum miñcātu; naṭaiyum miñcātu"
- ī.vi.riyu "ātici" āṅkila ākka muṉṉuraiyil) 
"moḻipeyarppu eṉpatu eṉṉa? 
piṟa moḻippāvalaṉiṉ aṟupaṭṭatalai - oru taṭṭil; 
kiḷiyiṉ pēttal; kuraṅkiṉ kūccal 
iṟantavaṉukku iḻivu. 				- viḷātimir nāpakāv
"moḻipeyarppu mūlattai muḻumaiyākak kāṭṭa vallatalla eṉpatu aṉaivarum aṟintatē eṉiṉum ulaka naṭappil ilakkiyappaṇikaḷil (taramāṉa) moḻipeyarp pukkum oru talaiyāya iṭam uṇṭu"
- katē 
5. muṉaivar vi. irāmacāmiyuṭaiya "tirukkuṟaḷai . moḻipeyartta l"* (On Translating Thirukural: 2001) nūl kaḻaka ilakkiyattai āṅkilattil moḻi peyarppatil eḻum cikkalkaḷaic cevvaṉē viḷakkukiṟatu; avaṟṟai iyaṉṟavarai tavirppataṟkāṉa vaḻimuṟaikaḷaiyum tarukiṟatu. avar virivāṉa āyvukku eṭuttuk koṇṭa tirukkuṟaḷ āṅkila ākkaṅkaḷ varumāṟu:

1987 pi. es. cuntaram

6. iṟutiyākap piṉvarum puṟanāṉūṟṟup pāṭalkaḷai, atāvatu
192. "yātum ūrē.'' 
252. " kaṟaṅku veḷḷaruvi '' 
271. ''nīraṟavu aṟiyā..'' 
159. "vāḻum nāḷōṭu.''
188. "paṭaippup pala paṭaittu.'' 
ākiya aintil ovvoṉṟukkum pōp, mār, irāmāṉujaṉ, hārṭ mutaliyōr ceyta palvēṟu moḻipeyarppukaḷiṉ niṟaikuṟaikaḷai pi. marutanāyakam (2003) kaṭṭuraiyil nayampaṭa āyvu ceytuḷḷār atuvum kaḻaka ilakkiya moḻipeyarppukkuc ciṟanta vaḻikāṭṭiyākum. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka 192m pāṭalum ataṉ āṅkila ākkaṅkaḷum ippakutiyiṉ aṭiyil tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. avai kuṟitta kaṭṭuraiyāciriyar karuttukaḷ varumāṟu: 
pōp cāṉaṭ (Sonnet) eṉṉum kaṭiṉamāṉa pāviṉattil, īṟṟetukaikaḷuṭaṉ, mūlattiṉ poruḷ māṟāmal āṅkilap paṭuttiyuḷḷār. (īṟṟetukaikkākaveṉṟē vantatu When joyous life seems like a luscious draught) eṉpa tu "vāḻtal iṉitu'' eṉpatai "uyarkuṭippuk kuṭittuk kaḷittal pōṉṟa iṉiya vāḻkkai" eṉṟu etukaikkāka māṟṟiviṭṭār ātāramiṉṟi!)
mār: "tītum naṉṟum piṟar tara vārā" eṉpatai " I receive Wrong doing as though it were good' (= tīmai ceyyappaṭṭālum atai nāṉ naṉṟākak karuti ēṟpēṉ) eṉa apattamāka māṟṟi viṭṭār. 'nōtalum taṇitalum avaṟṟōraṉṉa' eṉpatai "Pain and relief from pain, are as one" (= nōvum oṉṟu tāṉ nōvu nīṅkuvatum oṉṟu tāṉ) eṉa āṅkilappaṭutti yuḷḷatum cariyalla (nōtal taṇital ākiyavaiyum piṟar tara varuvaṉavalla eṉpatallavā poruḷ). "The cool drops of rain that fill with lightnings flash are not the same as it is” 6TOOTM toṭariyam pāṭaliṉ "miṉṉoṭu ..yāṟṟu' eṉṉum pakutiyaip puriyāmal ceyta piḻai. kaṭaici iraṇṭu varikaḷiṉ āṅkila vaṭivil foolish eṉṟa kaṭuñcollai āḷvatu mūlattiṟku etirāṉatu.
irāmāṉucaṉ: vaḻakkampōlac ciṟappākavē aṟputamāka āṅkilap paṭuttiyuḷḷār. puṟappāṭaliṉ karuttu muḻumaiyāka uḷḷatu; nayamāṉa āṅkila naṭai; eṉiṉum oru ciṟukuṟai: ''ciṟiyōrai ikaḻtal ataṉiṉum ilamē' eṉpuḻi ataṉiṉum eṉṉum colliṉ nalattai maṟantuviṭṭār. (pōp maṟakkavillai : Still less despise we eṉṟu kavaṉamāka eḻutiṉār!)
hārṭ & haipeṭs: hārṭṭiṉ tāymoḻi āṅ kilam ; tamiḻilum camaṟkirutattilum āḻnta pulamaiyuḷḷavar; avarukku utaviyavar āṅkilap pāvalar haipeṭs. eṉavē ivviruvariṉ āṅkila ākkam muntaiya mūvar ākkaṅkaḷai viṭac ciṟappākavuḷḷatu; mūlattiṉ poruḷai muḻumaiyāka, teḷivāka appaṭiyē kāṭṭukiṟatu -- pānayamāṉatu pōp, irāmāṉucaṉ ākiyōr ākkaṅkaḷai viṭac caṟṟē māṟṟuk kuṟaivāka iruppiṉum. āruyir = ariya uyir ; itaṉai "Our lives, however dear'' eṉṟār irāmāṉucaṉ ; a life with its hardships eṉṟār hārṭ ; pāṭaliṉ nōkkukku iccollai hārṭ moḻi peyarttatē poruttamāṉatu.
yātum ūrē, yāvarum kēḷir 
tītum naṉṟum piṟartara vārā 
nōtalum taṇitalum avaṟṟō raṉṉa; 
cātalum putuvatu aṉṟē ; vāḻtal 
iṉiteṉa makiḻntaṉṟum ilamē; muṉiviṉ 
iṉṉātu eṉṟalum ilamē; miṉṉoṭu 
vāṉam taṇtuḷi talaii āṉātu 
kalporutu iṟaṅkum mallal pēr yāṟṟu 
nīrvaḻip paṭūum puṇaipōl, ār uyir 
muṟaivaḻip paṭūum eṉpatu tiṟavōr 
kāṭciyil teḷintaṉam ākaliṉ, māṭciyiṉ 
periyōrai viyattalum ilamē 
ciṟiyōrai ikaḻtal ataṉiṉum ilamē.
kaṇiyaṉ pūṅkuṉṟaṉ : puṟam 192 
To us all towns are one, all men our kin,
Life's good comes not from others' gift, nor ill; 
Man's pains and pains' relief are from within,
Death's no new thing; nor do our bosoms thrill 
When joyous life seems like a luscious draught.
When grieved, we patient suffer, for we deem 
This much praised life of ours a fragile raft
Borne down the waters of some mountain stream 
That O'er huge boulders roaring seeks the plain
Tho'storms with lightnings' flash from darken'd skies 
Descend the raft goes on as fates ordain.
Thus have we seen in visions of the wise! 
We marvel not at greatness of the great; 
Still less despise we men of low estate.
- G. U. Pope 191Q 
All villages are mine and every one my friend 
I receive wrong doing as though it were good, 
and do not pay it back. Pain and relief 
from pain are as one. To die is not new, 
while not to rejoice because life is sweet is 
absurd. Poor is the mind that holds it wrong 
to seek after something. The cool drops of rain 
that fall with the lightning's flash are not the same 
as it is. Like the waters of the stream that rushes 
among rocks is sweet life, set down on the pre-ordained 
path. Since this has clearly been foreseen by 
the wise it is foolish to extol the greatness of 
the great or to censure the lowly.
J. R. Marr 1958/1985 
Every town our home town, 
every man a kinsman 
Good and evil do not come 
from others 
Pain and relief of pain 
come of themselves. 
Dying is nothing new. 
We do not rejoice 
that life is sweet 
nor in anger 
call it bitter. 
Our lives, however dear, 
follow their own course
rafts drifting 
in the rapids of a great river 
sounding and dashing over the rocks 
after a downpour
from skies slashed by lightnings 
We know this 
from the vision 
of men who see. 
So, 
We are not amazed by the great,
and we do not scorn the little
- A. K. Ramanujan (1985) 
Every city is your city. Everyone is your kin, 
Failure and prosperity do not come to you because others 
have sent them! Nor do suffering and the end of suffering. 
There is nothing new in death. Thinking that living 
is sweet, we do not rejoice in it. Even less do we say 
if something unwanted happens, that to live is miserable!
Through the vision of those who have understood we know 
that a life, with its hardsdhip, makes it way like a raft 
riding the water of a huge and powerful river roaring
Without pasue as it breaks against rocksbecause the clouds 
crowded with bolts of lightning pour down their cold 
drops of the rain, and so we are not amazed 
at those who are great and even less do we despise the weak
- G. L. Hart & Heifetz (1999) 
7. mēlē nāṉkām pattiyiṟ kaṇṭaṉavaṟṟiṉ āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ iṉṟiyamaiyātavai; avai varumāṟu:
“The details are not so important; it is better to keep truly and devotedly the essence and nature of the poet's metaphors than to cling to trifles in a servile way and, at the same time, to lose the spirit and nature of the whole”
K. V. Zvelebil (1956) 
Language learning and the study of literature are two completely different things, and translation has to be a part of the latter if it is to receive the breath that is inherent to it. XXXXX a student is led into language study through literature and not the reverse. There are those who begin Greek after reading Aeschylus XXXXX
So, the translator must be a writer above all, with the instincts and drives that go to make a writer.”
- Gregory Rabassa in Frawley (1984) 
All translation is a kind of illusion, more or less perfect according to circumstances and varying also with the skill of the translator... Where there exists in English a corresponding form and manner a high degree of illusion is possible. Those translations are always best in which the illusion is most complete and the idiom least suggestive of translation"
Higham (1938) 
Poetry, indeed, cannot be translated, and therefore, it is the poets that preserve languages; for we would not be at the touble to learn a language, if we would have all that is written in it just as well in a translation. But as the beauties of poetry cannot be preserved in any language except that in which it was originally written, we learn the language."
- Dr Samuel Johnson
“ It is useless to read Greek in translation. Translators can but offer us a vague equivalent”
- Virginia wolf. 
“If we put Homer straight into English words neither meaning nor manner survives”
- E. V. Rieu (in his preface to his translation
of Homer's Odyssey)
 “The only purpose of education is to enable one to read Homer in the original Greek”
- Sheppard (1949)
 “What is translation? On a platter 
A poet's pale and glaring head 
A parrot's speech, a monkey's chatter 
And profanation of the dead.
- Vladimir Nabakov
 “Say what one will of its inadequacy, translation remains one of the most important worthwhile concerns in the totality of world affairs"
- Goethe

iyal 5
tamiḻiṉ toṉmai : piṟa aṟivup
pulaṅkaḷuṭaṉ iṇainta āyvu
(cintuveḷi nākarika akaḻvāyvu, annākarika
eḻuttāyvu uṭpaṭa)
tamiḻarkaḷ ulakil iṉṟuḷḷa maṟṟa enta moḻi, paṇ pāṭṭaiyum viṭa atikat toṉmaiyāṉatum iṭaiyīṭu illātatum āṉa moḻi, paṇpāṭṭiṉ piṟaṅkaṭaikaḷ āvar. mānta iṉa nākarika vaḷarcciyiṉ mikac ciṟanta iyalkaḷil oṉṟu tamiḻar marapuc celvam ākum. iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷil māntaṉ toṉmoḻikku mika neruṅkiyatu tamiḻ maṭṭumē. "mutal tāymoḻi" āyvil īṭupaṭṭuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷukku oḷi taravallatum tamiḻē. oru ciṟu nilapparappiṉ varalāṟu, paṇpāṭu ākiyavaṟṟaic cariyākap purintu koḷḷa ippaṭi mānta iṉattiṉ oṭṭumotta iṉa, moḻi varalāṟu kaḷuṭaṉ toṭarpupaṭuttip pārkka vēṇṭiya nilaiyil uḷḷa (tamiḻakam pōṉṟa pakutikaḷ ulakil mikac cilavē; tamiḻ taṉit taṉmai vāyntatu. es ē ṭailar kūṟiyuḷḷatu pōl intiya nākarikattiṉ aṭittaḷam tirāviṭa (tamiḻ) moḻi - paṇpāṭu ākiyavaiyē (S.A. Tyler. "India, m Anthropological perspective (1973) "All of Indian civilization is built on an underlying base of Dravidian language and culture') intiya varalāṟṟu āyvu kaḷukkum tamiḻ cārnta āyvukaḷ mika iṉṟiyamaiyātavai.
2. tamiḻ eṉṉum colliṉ tiripē tirāviṭam (tamiḻ, tamiḷ, tramiḷa , trāmiṭa, trāviṭa). tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam eṉa aḻaippatu tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpattaiyē. tol tirāviṭam (Proto-Dravidian) eṉṟu vaṇṇa ṉai moḻi nūlāyvāḷar mīṭṭuruvākkam ceyvaṉavaṟṟuḷ pala piḻaiyāy muṭikiṉṟaṉa eṉpatum, paḻantamiḻē itaṟkuk kaṭṭaḷaik kallākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum eṉpatum, pāvāṇar koḷkai . tamiḻiṉ tiripukaḷē piṟa tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ. paḻan tamiḻiṉiṉṟum vēṟupaṭṭa "tol tirāviṭam' eṉpatu kaṟpaṉaiyē eṉpar pāvāṇar. ākavē tirāviṭam, tol tirāviṭam eṉa innūlil kuṟippiṭappaṭuvaṉavellām uṇmaiyil paḻantamiḻaiyē kuṟippatākak koḷka.
3. tamiḻ, tamiḻar varalāṟṟaic cevvaṉē aṟiya māntar iṉat tōṟṟamum paravalum; moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum paṟṟi iṉṟaiya paṉṉāṭṭu aṟiñar ēṟṟuḷḷa karuttukkaḷait terintu koḷvatu iṉṟiyamaiyātatu. avaṟṟai aṭuttu varum pakutikaḷil kāṇpōm. 
ka. mānta iṉattōṟṟamum paravalum
4. puṭaviyiṉ (pirapañcattiṉ) akavai cumār 1400 kōṭi āṇṭuvarai irukkalām. ulakam uṭpaṭa ñāyiṟṟuk kuṭum pattiṉ akavai ēṟattāḻa 450 kōṭi āṇṭu ākum. ulakil mutaṉ mutalil uyirkaḷ tōṉṟiyatu ēṟattāḻa 300 kōṭi āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉartāṉ. mutalil tōṉṟiyavai 'pākṭīriyā' pōṉṟa ōraṇu uyirikaḷē. iṉṟuḷḷa aṉaittuc ceṭi koṭikaḷum vilaṅku - paṟavaikaḷum orē mūlattil tōṉṟiyavaiyē ākum. ivvuyiriṉa vakaikaḷil 15 ilakkam taṉi iṉaṅkaḷ (Species) kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭup peyar cūṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. iṉṉum iṉam kaṇṭupiṭikkap paṭātavaiyum, peyar cūṭṭappaṭātavaiyum (kuṟippāka nilaittiṇai, nuṇṇuyirkaḷ, ciṟṟuyirkaḷ) mummaṭaṅku irukkum eṉṟu aṟiviya lārkaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉar. iṉṟaikku 24 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar oru taṭavaiyum, āṟaraik kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar maṟṟoru taṭavaiyum pēraḻivukaḷ ēṟpaṭṭu antantak kālakkaṭṭattil irunta uyiriṉaṅkaḷuḷ perumpālāṉavai aḻintuviṭṭaṉa. (mikap periya viṇ koḷḷikaḷ (Meteors) ulakil viḻuntatāl appēraḻivukaḷ ēṟpaṭṭirukkalām eṉkiṉṟaṉar). ippoḻutu uḷḷa pālūṭṭikaḷ (Mammals) aṉaittumē 16 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvāṉa oru ciṟiya eli pōṉṟa parumaṉuṭaiya) vilaṅkiliruntu paṭimalarcci aṭaintavaiyē. kuraṅkukaḷukkum māntaṉukkum mūtātai yāṉa ilemūr (Lemur) vilaṅku (ēṟattāḻa 250 kirām eṭai) uruvāṉa kālam āṟaraik kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar ākum. (pūccikaḷ tōṉṟi 50 kōṭi āṇṭum eṟumpukaḷ tēṉīkkaḷ tōṉṟi 10 kōṭi āṇṭum, kaṟaiyāṉ tōṉṟi 28 kōṭi āṇṭum ākiṉṟaṉa) ip pakkattiṟkum aṭutta pakkattukkum iṭaiyil oṭṭiyuḷḷa 'puviyiṉ tōṟṟattiliruntu ikkāla māntaṉ varai' eṉṟa vivarap paṭattai kāṇka.
5. māntaṉ eppaṭit tōṉṟiṉāṉ? māntak kuraṅkiṉat tukkum (cimpaṉci, korillā) māntaṉukkum potuvāṉa vēṟu ōr uyiriṉam iṉṟaikku ēṟattāḻa 50 ilakkam āṇ ṭukku muṉ āppirikkāvil iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu aṟiviyal muṭivu. iṉṟu ulakeṅkum uḷḷa 650 kōṭi māntarkaḷumē (atāvatu tirāviṭar, intō airōp piyar, maṅkōliyar, cemittiyar, amerikka intiyar ākiya aṉaivarumē) hōmō cēpiyaṉs cēpiyaṉs allatu ikkāla māntar (Home Sapiens Sapiens or Anatomically Modern Humans) eṉṉum orē iṉattaic cārntavarkaḷ. ivviṉam āppirikkāvil iṉṟaikku oṉṟarai ilakkam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar vāḻnta orē tāyiṭam iruntu tōṉṟiyatu eṉpatu iṉṟaiya aṟiviyalār aṉaivarum ēṟṟa muṭivu (Cavalli Sforza and others: The history and geography of Human genes, 1994). ēṟattāḻa māntaṉaiyotta "muṉmānta (Hominid) " iṉaṅkaḷ kaṭanta 48 ilakkam āṇṭukaḷil tōṉṟic cila pala ilakkam 1 āṇṭukaḷ vāḻntu piṉṉar muṟṟilum aḻintoḻintu viṭṭaṉa. avaṟṟuḷ hōmō erakṭas eṉṟa iṉamum aṭaṅkum. atu maṭṭumē āppirikkāvai viṭṭu veḷiyēṟip piṟa kaṇṭaṅkaḷilum paraviyiruntatu. kurōmakṉāṉ maṉitaṉ, pīkiṅ māntaṉ, cāvakamāntaṉ, attirampākkam pācil māntaṉ ākiyavarkaḷ (ēṟattāḻa 3 ilaṭcam āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar vāḻntavarkaḷ) inta hōmō erakṭs vakaiyaic cārntavarkaḷē. airōppāvil 40,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ varai vāḻntu piṉṉar aṭiyōṭu aḻikkappaṭṭuviṭṭa niyāṉṭartal (Neandarthal) iṉamum muṉmānta iṉamē. inta "muṉmānta iṉaṅkaḷ " evaṟṟiṭaiyēyum "moḻi" uruvākavē illai (None of them had the faculty of speech)
6. ippoḻutuḷḷa māntarkaḷākiya AMH vakaiyaic cārnta nam mānta iṉam, kaṭanta oṉṟarai ilakkam āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉar āppirikkāvil tōṉṟi ulakeṅkum piṉ varumāṟu paraviyatu - atāvatu,

 paraviṉar eṉpatu vallunar karuttu ākum. itai viḷakkum nilappaṭam aṭutta pakkattil uḷḷatu. kaṇṭaṅkaḷ nakarvuk koḷkaiyiṉ paṭi (Continental drift) kaṇṭaṅkaḷ kaṭanta 25 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷākap pirintu nakarntuḷḷaṉa. eṉiṉum ulakil iṉṟu uḷḷa kaṇṭaṅkaḷ ellām ēṟattāḻa ippō tuḷḷa uruvai ēṟattāḻa oru kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉarē aṭaintuviṭṭaṉa. ataṟkup piṉṉar, kaṇṭam aḷavukku (Continental proportions) peru nilappakuti etuvum kaṭalukkuḷ mūḻkavillai . āyiṉum paṉiyūḻi muṭivil ki.mu. 8000 vākkil (paṉikkaṭṭi urukik kaṭal maṭṭam cumār 300 aṭi uyarntatāl ulakeṅkum kaṭalōrap pakutiyākiya kaṇṭat tiṭṭu (Continental shelf) ēṟattāḻa - irunūṟu kal aḷavukku kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyatu. avvāṟu akkālak kaṭṭattil tamiḻakattaic cuṟṟiyum (teṉ ticai uṭpaṭa) cila nūṟu kal (mail) tolaivum kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyirukkalām. avvāṟu mūḻkiya nilappakutiyaiyē kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷum kaḷaviyal uraiyum cuṭṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatē iṉṟaiya aṟiviyalukkup poruntuvatākum. āppirikkāviliruntu ikkāla māntaṉ ēṟattāḻa 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar āttirēliyā, mēṟku āciyā pōṉṟa pakutikaḷukkup pulam peyarntu ceṉṟatu teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyāka irukkalām. jiyākirāpikal mākacīṉ cepṭempar 2006 itaḻil ikkālamāntar āppirikkāvai viṭṭu teṉṉintiyāvaiyoṭṭiya kaṇṭattiṭṭu vaḻi yākap pulampeyarntu ceṉṟa pātaiyaik kāṭṭum nilap paṭam uḷḷatu. akkālak kaṭṭattil ippoḻutu uḷḷatu pōlavē ellāk kaṇṭaṅkaḷum iruntaṉa veṉiṉum nilap pakuti cila nūṟukal virivāka iruntirukkum. intiyak karai 


cārnta kaṇṭattiṭṭup pakutiyil atāvatu karaiyōrak kaṭaṟ pakutiyil (Continental Shelf) āḻkaṭal akaḻāyvu ceytāl itu paṟṟiya cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭaikkalām eṉṟu kūṟukiṟār piḷemiṅ (2004). iṉṟaiya māntaṉiṭam uḷḷavai 23x2 'kurōmōcōmkaḷ. avaṟṟil aṭaṅkiya marapaṇukkaḷ (Genomes) eṇṇikkai 30,000. namatu marapaṇukkaḷukkum māntakkuraṅku marapaṇukkaḷukkum 98.50 viḻukkāṭu oṟṟumai uṇṭu. (oṉṟarai viḻukkāṭē marapaṇu nilaiyil māntak kuraṅkiliruntu māntaṉ vēṟupaṭṭavaṉ eṉṟālum māntaṉ evvaḷavu māperum koṭumaiyāṉa uyiriyāka māṟi viṭṭāṉ!)
7. kaṇṭaṅkaḷ nakarvuk koḷkai, maṟṟum kaṇṭaṅkaḷiṉ kaṭalōrap pakutikaḷ kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiya ceyti ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil kaḻaka nūlkaḷiṟ kāṇum kaṭalkōḷ ceyti kaḷai iṉṟaiya aṟiviyalukkēṟpa viḷakka vēṇṭiyuḷḷatu. kumari muṉaikkut teṟkil nilappakutikaḷ kaṭalkōḷil mūḻkiya ceytiyaik kalittokai 104m cilappatikāram kāṭu kāṇkātaiyum kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. tolkāppiyattiṉ mutal uraikārar ākiya (ki.pi. 10 ām nūṟṟāṇṭaic cārnta) iḷampūraṇarum itaik kuṟippiṭukiṟār. avarukkup piṉṉar vanta iṟaiyaṉār akapporuḷ urai yāciriyarum aṭiyārkku nallārum icceytiyai mēlum virivupaṭuttic caṟṟu mikaippaṭuttik kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. paṇṭait tamiḻilakkiyam kūṟum ikkaṭalkōḷ ceytiyaip paṟṟi eḻutappaṭṭuḷḷa āyvuraikaḷ varumāṟu: 
i) ca. cōmacuntara pārati (1913) tamiḻp paṇṭai ilakkiyaṅkaḷum tamiḻakamum, cittānta tīpikā XIV 
ii)	vi.jē. tampi piḷḷai (1913) : māṇikkavācakar toṉma varalāṟu, tamiḻiyaṉ āṇṭikuvāri II – 
iii)	 maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ (1930) : māṇikkavācakar varalāṟum kālamum 
iv) 	ē. es. vaittiyanāta aiyar (1929) : "kīḻaināṭukaḷiṉ

catapata pirāmaṇam 18 mutaliyavaṟṟil kūṟappaṭum "maṉu piraḷayam" tirāviṭat toṉmattiliruntu uruvākiyatu eṉa (iii) um (iv) um kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. cumēriyap piraḷayak kataikūṭap paḻan tamiḻk kaṭalkōḷ toṉmattiliruntu uruppeṟṟatē eṉa (1) m (vi)m karutukiṉṟaṉa.
8. intiya mākkaṭalil paḻaṅkālattil oru kaṇṭam iruntu atu kaṭalil mūḻki viṭṭatu eṉṟu 19 ām nūṟṟāṇṭu iṟutiyil ēkkalum vēṟu cilarum karuti ataṟku "lemūriyā" eṉṟa peyaraiyum iṭṭaṉar. āṉāl iṉṟuḷḷa aṟiviya laṟiñarkaḷiṉ orumitta karuttu eṉṉa? kaṇṭam aḷavukkup periya nilapparappu etuvum entak kālattilum kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiṭa villai eṉpatē avarkaḷ muṭivu eṉak kaṇṭōm. kāntira tāv tamatu "mukkaṭaṟputirkaḷ" nūlil itai 1974lēyē cuṭṭiyuḷḷār. eṉiṉum itai yārum kaṇṭukoḷvatillai!
"toṉmai nākarikaṅkaḷaip paṭaitta mānta iṉaṅ kaḷaik koṇṭirunta (kaṇṭam pōṉṟa ) perunilapparappukaḷ evaiyum entak kālattilum intiyā, pacipik, aṭlāṇṭik peruṅkaṭalkaḷ evaṟṟilum iruntiruppataṟkāṉa vāyppu aṟavē illai eṉalām".
"It is highly improbable that big land masses, inhabited by large numbers of people who created ancient civilisations ever existed in the Indian, Pacific Or Atlantic Oceans". (p267)
ippoḻutu aṟiviyal ēṟṟuḷḷatu kaṇṭa nakarvu maṟṟum nilappāḷaṅkaḷ kōṭpāṭē (Continental drift and Plate techtonics) yākum. itaṉai viḷakkum ulakap paṭaṅkaḷ iraṇṭai aṭutta iru pakkaṅkaḷ il kāṇka. aṭutta pakkaṅkaḷil. āyiṉum ki.mu. 8,000ai oṭṭi uram (WURM) paṉiyūḻi iṟutiyil kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu ulakeṅkum ēṟattāḻa irunūṟu, munnūṟu kal aḷavukku kaṭaṟkaraip pakuti kaṭalil mūḻkiviṭṭatu eṉpatai ipputiya kōṭpāṭum ēṟṟuk koḷkiṟatu. akkaṭalkōḷ kālattiṟku muṉṉar intup peruṅkaṭalil uḷḷa tīvukaḷ mūlamāka āppirikkāvaiyum teṉṉintiyāvaiyum iṇaitta vāl pōṉṟa nilappakuti kaḷum cila (piṉṉar mūḻki viṭṭavai) iruntirukkalām eṉavum kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. (vālṭar pēr carvīs ; The Roots of Ancient India, 1971). ilemūriyāk kaṇṭak koḷkai nilavi vanta 




kālattilum kūṭa ca. cōmacuntara pārati, mayilai cīṉi vēṅkaṭacāmi, em ārōkkiyacāmi pōṉṟa ciṟanta aṟiñarkaḷ kumarikkut teṟkil kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiya pakuti kaṇṭam aḷaviṉa taṉṟu; ciṟu nilappakutiyāka irukkalām eṉṟē kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷaṉar. kā. appātturaiyārum 'kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu' eṉṟu kuṟippiṭṭatai uṇarka. ippoḻutu ilemūriyāk kaṇṭak koḷkai aṭippaṭaiyaṟṟatu eṉṟu niṟuvappaṭṭu viṭṭataic cākkāka vaittuk koṇṭu cumati irāmacāmi tamatu kaṟpaṉaip puviyiyalāḷarkaḷum, pēraḻivu varalāṟu kaḷum; mūḻkiviṭṭa ilemūriyā : Fabulous geographers, catastrophic Histories : The Lost Lemuria (2004: permaṉeṉṭ piḷāk, ṭelli) eṉṉum nūlil "kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu " paṟṟi eḻutiya tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷ aṉaivaraiyum tākki ēḷaṉam ceytuḷḷār. 1950kaḷukkup piṉ "lemūriyāk kaṇṭak" koḷkaiyai aṟiviyal aṟavē kaiviṭṭu viṭṭatu eṉpatu uṇmaitāṉ. āṉāl cumati irāmacāmi oṉṟaik kuṟippiṭa maṟukkiṟār. atu eṉṉa? kalittokai, cilappatikāram, piṟkāla uraiyāciriyar nūlkaḷ cuṭṭum kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu ciṟiya aḷaviṉatāka iruntirukkalām eṉṟu tamiḻaka aracu 1972 il veḷiyiṭṭa tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu - tol paḻaṅkālam pōṉṟa nūlkaḷ kuṟippiṭuvatai avar kaṇṭu koḷḷavillai. aṉṟaiya 'nāṭu' eṉpatu intiyā' tamiḻ nāṭu' pōṉṟa perunilap pakutiyaṉṟu. iṉṟaiya vaṭṭa (tālukā) aḷaviṟkuḷ kūṭa iraṇṭu - mūṉṟu nāṭukaḷ uḷḷaṉavē. uraiyāciriyarkaḷ kuṟitta 49 nāṭukaḷum cērntu kaṇṭam aḷavukku iruntirukka vēṇṭiyatēyillai. muṉ pattiyil kūṟiyatu pōla ki.mu. 8,000ai oṭṭik kaṇṭattiṭṭuppakuti kaṭalil mūḻkiyataiyē kaḻaka nūlkaḷil uḷḷa kaṭalkōḷ ceytikaḷ kūṟuvatākak koṇṭāl iṉṟaiya aṟiviyal kaṇṭupiṭippukaḷukkuk kaḻaka (caṅka) ilakkiyak kaṭalkōḷ ceyti muṟṟilum icaivatēyākum eṉpatu tēṟṟam. (kāṇka pi. irāmanātaṉ 1998, 2003 2004).
9. ivvāṟu tamiḻ, tamiḻar ceytikaḷaik koccaip paṭutti eḻutuvatu ivvāciriyarukku vaḻakkamāṉatē. ti intiyaṉ ekaṉāmik aṇṭ cōciyal hisṭari riviyū 38-2 ēpral - cūṉ 2001 itaḻil cumati irāmacāmi "iṉaṅkaḷiṉ eccaṅkaḷ : tolliyal (tamiḻt tēciya uṇarvu, cintuveḷi nākarikattiṉpāl īrppu" eṉṟu oru kaṭṭurai eḻutiṉār. cintu veḷi nākarikamum annākarikamuttirai eḻuttukaḷum tirāviṭam (tol tamiḻ ) cārntavaiyē eṉṟu ciṟanta āyvāḷarkaḷ kamil cuvelapil (1990: tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟimukam, iyal 6: tirāviṭa moḻikaḷum hārappā moḻiyum) askō parpōlā (praṇṭ laiṉ ak 24,2000) karutuvatai yellām ak kaṭṭuraiyil cumati irāmacāmi kaṇṭu koḷḷavillai. cintuveḷi nākarikattiṉ tamiḻac cārpai valiyuṟuttum tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷai yellām (parpōlā, pārcarvīs, cuvalepil pōṉṟavarkaḷ āyvukaḷiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil eḻutupavarkaḷ uṭpaṭa), avar eḷḷi nakaiyāṭiyuḷḷār.
u moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum
10. muṉmānta iṉaṅkaḷ aṉaittum pēccāṟṟal aṟṟavai, avarkaḷi ṭaiyē moḻi uruvākavillai, ēṟattāḻa 1% ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉāl tōṉṟiya ikkāla mānta iṉattiṭaiyē (AMH) maṭṭumē ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar moḻi uruvākiyatu eṉpatu iṉṟaiya palatuṟai aṟiviyalāḷariṉ orumitta muṭivākum.

12. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉaittum orē mutaṉ moḻiyiliruntu tōṉṟiyirukka vāyppuṇṭu eṉṟa koḷkai 19 ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ māksmullarukkum icai vāṉatē. 20 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil ñāla mutaṉ moḻi āyvil īṭupaṭṭa mēṉāṭṭaṟiñarkaḷ petarcaṉ, tirām petti, cuvā tecu, kirīṉperk, meriṭ rūlaṉ, illic cuvitic, tāl kōpālski, cevarōskiṉ, stārōstiṉ, pām hārṭu maṟṟum kerṉs jāṉ, peṅṭcaṉ, vāklāv pilācak pōṉṟa palarāvar. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷaiyum piṉvarumāṟu oru cila peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷukkuḷ (Super families) aṭakkalām eṉpatu avarkaḷ kaṇṭuḷḷa uṇmaiyākum.
i) nāstirāṭik (intō - airōppiyaṉ, tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, urālik, al tāyk, kārtvelliyaṉ, āprō - ēciyāṭik - atāvatu cemittiya - hāmittiyak kuṭumpam, ākiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ itil aṭaṅkum). kirīṉperk vakut tuḷḷa yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattukkum itaṟkum atika vēṟupāṭu illai. yūrēciyāṭik - kil aṭaṅkiyavai etrus kaṉ, intō - airōppiyaṉ, urālik - yūkākīr, al tāyk, koriyaṉ - cappāṉiyam - aiṉu, kilyāk, cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō - alyūt ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷākum.
(2000 akṭōparil sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalaikkaḻakattil pattiri rāj kiruṣṇamūrttiyai kirīṉperk cantitta poḻutu, "tirāviṭa moḻi yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattiṉ uṭaṉpiṟappāka irukkalām. makaḷāka irukka muṭiyātu" eṉat Og STJÓTTITII. (Bh. Krishnamurthi. The Dravidian Languages, 2003, pak. 46). itiliruntu slāv, irāṉiyaṉ, vētamoḻi camaskirutam, kirīk, ilattīṉ, keltik, cerumāṉiyam pōṉṟa moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷuk kellām tāyāṉa intō airōppiyattai viṭat toṉmai vāyntatu tirāviṭa moḻikkuṭumpam, atāvatu paḻantamiḻ eṉṟu uṇaralām.)
ii. cīṉa - kākēciyaṉ 
iii. āstirik (muṇṭā pōṉṟavai) 
iv. amerikka intiya moḻikaḷ 
V. intō - pacipik 
vi. koycāṉ
vii. kāṅkō - cakārā 
palvēṟu moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷai iṇaittu avaṟṟukku mūlamāṉa moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷaik kāṇum āyvāḷar kaḷuḷ cilar ataṟkum mēlē pōy ñāla mutaṉmoḻi  (māntaṉiṉ mutal tāymoḻi) āyvukkum ceṉṟuḷḷaṉar. meriṭ rūlaṉ taṉatu moḻikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam - moḻikaḷiṉ koṭivaḻi āyvu (sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalaikkaḻakam, 1994) nūliṉ pakkam 277 il kūṟuvatu varumāṟu: "perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷāviṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉaittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉar nilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyi liruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa cāṉṟu uḷḷatu" annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 277-366il 27 mutaṉmaiyāṉa karuttukaḷukkup palvēṟu moḻikkuṭumpaṅ kaḷilum uḷḷa coṟkaḷ "Global Etymologies" tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. akkaruttukkaḷukku ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyil eṉṉa vērc col iruntirukkalām eṉṟu ūkikka atu muṉṉōṭi yākum. avaṟṟuḷ palavaṟṟukku (ēṟattāḻa 17kkut tamiḻc coṟkaḷē poruḷ potintaṉa vākavum ñālamutaṉ moḻi yiṉ vērccol vaṭivai viḷakkuvaṉa vākavum amaintuḷḷaṉa.
13. irāparṭ kālṭuvel 1856il taṉatu tirāviṭa atāvatu teṉ intiya moḻikaḷiṉ oppilakkaṇam eṉ ṉum māperum nūlai veḷiyiṭṭār. (tiruntiya virivāṉa iraṇṭām patippu 1875) oru puṟam tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum maṟupuṟam,
intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷ 
cemittiya moḻikaḷ 
elāmaiṭ moḻi 
cittiya (ippoḻutu 'urāl - alṭāyk ) moḻikaḷ 
cappāṉiya moḻi
āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi moḻikaḷ 
ākiyavaṟṟukkum iṭaiyē kāṇum ilakkaṇa oppumaikaḷ, col oppumaikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟaip paṟṟi ērāḷamāṉa ceyti kaḷai avar tantār. immoḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ piriyumuṉṉar irunta (māntaṉ toṉ moḻiyiṉ) nilaiyai viḷakkuvataṟkāṉa oḷiyai ñāla mutaṉ moḻikku mika neruṅkiya tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattaic cārnta moḻikaḷiliruntutāṉ peṟṟāka vēṇṭum eṉpatai acceytikaḷ valuvāka niṟuviṉa. kālṭuvel kōṭiṭṭuk kāṭṭiyatai mēlum nuṇuki ārāyntu "mānta iṉa mutaṉ moḻi oṉṟākattāṉ iruntirukka vēṇṭum; mikat toṉmai vāynta toltamiḻē (tol tirāviṭam eṉa aḻaikka virumpuvār avvāṟē aḻaikkalām, maṟuppillai) aṉaittu moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum (intō-āriya moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) mūlamāṉa moḻiyākak karutappaṭa vēṇṭum" eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭai mēlum āḻamāka virivāka niṟuviyavarkaḷ nallūr ñāṉap pirakāca aṭikaḷum ñā. tēvanēyap pāvāṇarum āvar. intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpa moḻikaḷiṉ (ēṉ piṟamoḻikkuṭumpa moḻikaḷukkum tāṉ) aṭippaṭaic coṟkaḷ palavaṟṟukku ñāṉappirakācarum pāvāṇarum nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa poruttamāṉa tol - tirāviṭa vērc coṟkaḷai iṉam kāṭṭiyuḷḷaṉar. 1953il ñāṉappirakācar terivitta karuttu varumāṟu:
"intō airōppiya moḻikaḷil 'vēr kaḷ' eṉa uṉṉikkappaṭupavai poruḷ toṭarpaṟṟa veṟum kuṟiyīṭukaḷ tām. tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷō veṉil aiyattiṟkiṭamiṉṟip poruḷai yum kāraṇattaiyum kāṭṭupavai yāka uyirōṭṭamuḷḷa muḷaikkaru pōlat tōṉṟum; entak karut tōṭṭattil palvēṟu coṟkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatait teṟṟeṉa avai kāṭṭum vakaiyil amaintuḷḷaṉa. ittaṉmai avaṟṟukku il lātatāl intō airōppiya "vērkaḷ" iruḷ maṇṭiya poruḷaṟṟa veṟum olik kuviyalkaḷākavē tōṉṟum. avaṟṟukkum teḷivu tantu oḷi kāṭṭi uyirūṭṭam tarakkūṭiyavai tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷtām.''
piṟamoḻi vērkaḷukkum ikkaruttu perum aḷavukkup poruntuvatākum. ceṉṉaik kiṟittuvak kallūri itaḻil (1929) ñāṉappirakācar "moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟam" eṉṟa kaṭṭurai yil "intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ tantaiyākiya toṉ moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟattiṟkāṉa aṭippaṭaic cāṉṟukaḷ tamiḻiltāṉ kiṭṭum" eṉa muḻaṅkiyiruntār. (Tamil supplies this long looked for clue to finding the true origin of the proto Indo - European language)

15. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyiluḷḷa mika neruṅkiya oppumaiyai pi. irāmanātaṉ (kuppam) tirāviṭap palkalaik kaḻakattiṉ tirāviṭiyaṉ sṭaṭīs 1-3; ēpral - cūṉ 2003 itaḻil veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa kaṭṭurai virivāka niṟuvukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉ intiyāvil kaṇṭippāka 10,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉarē iruntu irukka vēṇṭum eṉ pataiyum iṅkiruntu ataṟku muṉṉarē āttirēliyap paḻaṅ kuṭi makkaḷ tol tamiḻ makkaḷiṭamiruntu pirintu ceṉṟirukka vēṇṭum eṉpataiyum akkaṭṭurai niṟuvukiṟatu.
ṅa. tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paraviyatu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakku nōkkiyē yākum; tamiḻar intiyāviṉ tolkuṭikaḷ.
16. ippoḻutu pala nāṭukaḷilumuḷḷa paltuṟai aṟiñar kaḷum potuvāka ēṟṟuk kaikkoṇṭuḷḷa (tavaṟāṉa) karutu kōḷiṉpaṭi tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar tāyakam vaṭa kiḻakku īrāṉ pakutiyākum ; aṅkiruntu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭi avarkaḷ intiyā, teṉṉintiyā, ilaṅkaiyai nōkki atāvatu teṟku nōkki iṭam peyarntu ceṉṟaṉar; cel lum vaḻiyil, pēruntu varum poḻutu āṅkāṅkuc cilar iṟakki viṭappaṭuvatu pōl, tirāviṭa moḻipēcum kuḻukkaḷ cila āṅkāṅku viṭṭu varappaṭṭaṉa. (kē.vi. cuvalepil (1972) : "tirāviṭarkaḷ iṟakkam " The descent of the Dravidians; tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal paṟṟiya paṉṉāṭṭu āyvitaḻ (IJDL) tokuti 2; pak. 57-63. pirittāṉiyak kalaik kaḷañciyattil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ paṟṟi avar eḻutiyuḷḷa kaṭṭuraiyilum avar ik karuttaiyē kūṟukiṟār. tamatu teṉṉintiya varalāṟu (4m patippu 1976) nūlil kē. ē. nīlakaṇṭa cāstiriyum itu "naṭantirukkak kūṭātataṉṟu" (not unlikely) eṉkiṟār.
17. itaṟku nērmāṟāṉa koḷkai tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar vaṭakku nōkkip paraviṉar eṉṉum "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam koḷkaiyākum : DRAVIDIAN ASCENT (from South). ippoḻutu atu paḻaiya pāṇik koḷkaiyākak karutap paṭiṉum ataṉai valiyuṟuttiyuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷuḷ her. ār. hāl; appar īrācu (hīrās pātiriyār), pi. ti. cīnivāca aiyaṅkār, vi. ār. irāmaccantira tīṭcitar, maṟaimalai aṭikaḷ, yu. ār. ehareṉpels, cēviyar .es taṉināyaka aṭikaḷār, ka.ta.tirunāvukkaracu, tēvanēyappāvāṇar, piriṭjeṭ maṟṟum irēmāṇṭ ālciṉ (1988), kē.kē.piḷḷai ākiyōrum aṭaṅkuvar. cuvirā jeysvāl (1974); jē. ār.mār (1975); parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ (1998) ākiyōr tirāviṭar ēṟṟam' eṉṟa koḷkaiyai ēṟkāviṭiṉum "tirāviṭar iṟakkam" eṉṟa koḷkaiyaiyum ēṟka maṟukkiṉṟaṉar.)
18. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭi intiyā vukkuḷ nuḻaintaṉar eṉṟa kōṭpāṭu mēlē pirivukaḷ (2) (ṅ) ākiyavaṟṟil coṉṉavaṟṟōṭu porunti varukiṟatā? moḻip peruṅ kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkiṭaiyēyuḷḷa varalāṟṟuk kālattukku muntiya uṟavukaḷaip paṟṟi āyvu ceyyum aṟiñar palar ittavaṟāṉa kōṭpāṭṭiṉai oṭpam iṉṟip piṉpaṟṟi varukiṉṟaṉar. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka ārpiḷeñc em.spiriks 1997 il tokuttu veḷiyiṭṭa tolliyalum moḻiyum : (1) kōṭpāṭṭu āyvuneṟik karuttōṭṭaṅkaḷ eṉṉum nūlil reṉpurū piṉvarum muṭivaik kūṟukiṟār:
"(naṭuk kiḻakkup pakutikaḷil iruntu vēḷāṇmait toḻil ceyta makkaḷ paṇṭu paravat toṭaṅkiyatu paṟṟiya) tolliyal cāṉṟu kaḷiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil tol intō airōp piyaṉ, tol āprō - ēciyāṭik, tol elāmaiṭ, tirāviṭam, tol alṭāyk moḻi kaḷ ākiya moḻikaḷaip pēciyōr aṉaivarum mikac curuṅkiya maiyak kiḻakku nilappakutiyil (cumēriyā, elām, iṉṟaiya turukki) uṭaṉ uṟaintu irukka vēṇṭum. immoḻikaḷ ellām (nāstirāṭik moḻiyiyalāḷar kūṟuvatu pōla) toṭarpuṭaiyavaiyāka iruppatu uṇmai yāṉāl avarkaḷellām appakutiyai viṭṭup pirintu vilakiya ki.mu. 8000 - 6000 kālakkaṭṭattukku muṉṉar oru cila āyiram āṇṭukaḷ immoḻikaḷ aṉaittiṟkum mūlamāṉa nāstirāṭik moḻi an nilappakutiyil pēcappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu tolliyal kaṇṭupiṭippukkaḷukkum ottu varumtarukkamākum.'' ivvāṟu tol - nāstirāṭik pēciyavarkaḷ aṉai varum 10,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar oruṅkē naṭuk kiḻakkup pakutiyil vāḻntiruntārkaḷ eṉṟa intak kōṭpāṭṭaiyum (ataṉ toṭarpāṉa "intiyāvukkuḷ aṅkiruntu tirāviṭar kaḷ iṟaṅkiyatu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭittāṉ" eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭaiyum poyppippatu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷukkum iṭaiyē uḷḷa neruṅkiya piṇaippu ākum. itupaṟṟi kāliṉ pi. mācikā 1999 il kūṟiyatu kuṟippiṭat takkatu.
"teṉ āciyāvil kaḻipaḻaṅkālattil oru potuvāṉa mūtātai moḻi iruntirukka vēṇ ṭum. ippōtaiya mānta iṉam (ēṟattāḻa ōr ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar) ulakeṅkum paravat toṭaṅkiya kāla kaṭṭamē tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiṉ toṭakkak kālam ākalām" (It may be a question of a very ancient common substratum in south Asia, Pre-Dravidian going back even to the original peopling of the world; The year Book of South Asian Languages and Linguistics, 2001 New Delhi).
19. ēṟattāḻa oṉṟarai ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉart ikkāla mānta iṉam āppirikkāvil tōṉṟiyatu; mutal tāymoḻi ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvāṉatu; iṉṟaikku ōr ilaṭcam - 50,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntu teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyākavum ulakiṉ pala pakutikaḷukkum ikkāla mānta iṉam paraviyatu eṉṟum mēlē kaṇṭōm. ivvāṟu vaṭakku, vaṭakiḻakku nōkkiya mānta iṉap paravalil tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunarukku mukāmaiyāṉa paṅku iruntirukka vēṇṭum. tirāviṭar ēṟṟam (Dravidian ascent) paṟṟiya intak kōṭpāṭṭai "ñāṉappirakācar - tēva nēyaṉ kōṭpāṭu" eṉa aḻaikkalām. (tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷukku āttirē liyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷuṭaṉ uḷḷa neruṅkiya toṭarpaiyum (moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ piṟa vaṟṟuṭaṉ uḷḷa neṅkiya toṭarpaiyum viḷakka vallatu ik kōṭpāṭēyākum. teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭakkē ceṉṟa tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar uruvākkiyatē cintuveḷi nākarika mākum. (hīrās 1953; mativāṇaṉ 1995 ; irāmanātaṉ 1999; pūraṇa cantirajīvā 2004) avarkaḷukku elām, cumēriyā, ekiptu mutaliya nākarikaṅkaḷai uruvākkiyatilum paṅku iruntirukka vēṇṭum.
20. cuvelapil (1990) "ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉar tirāviṭam, urāl - alṭāyk, cappāṉiyam moḻi pēcunar kaḷiṭaiyē neruṅkiya toṭarpu iruntirukka vēṇṭum" eṉṟu karutuvataik kaṇṭōm. attakaiya toṭarpaiyum "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam" eṉṉum kōṭpāṭē viḷakkavallatu. ñāṉappirakācar - tēvanēyaṉ kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi ki.mu. 10,000kku muṉṉarē tol intō - airōppiyam pēcunar tol - tirāviṭam pēcu nariṭamiruntu pirintu viṭṭaṉar; naṭu āciya sṭeppi pul veḷikaḷil cila āṇṭukaḷ vāḻntaṉar; avarkaḷil cila kuḻuviṉar ki.mu. 4000-3000 aḷavil mēṟku nōkki airōppāvukkuc ceṉṟaṉar. (avarkaḷiṭam piṉṉar uruvāṉavaiyē kirēkkam, ilattīṉ, kelṭik, jermāṉik, slāviya moḻik kuṭumpaṅ kaḷākum); vēṟu cila kuḻukkaḷ kiḻakku - teṉkiḻakku nōkki iṭam peyarnta kālam ki.mu. 2500ai oṭṭi ākum; avvāṟu kiḻakkē vanta kuḻuviṉar moḻikaḷil atāvatu intō āriya, irāṉiya moḻikaḷil paṇṭu (atāvatu ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉart tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiṉariṭa miruntu pirinta kālat til) uṭaṉ koṇṭu ceṉṟa tol tirāviṭa moḻik kūṟu kaḷōṭu cērttu, vaṭamēṟku intiyāvilum appālum vāḻntu vanta tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunarkaḷiṭam iruntu (iraṇ ṭāvatu kaṭṭamāka) putitāka mēlum pala tirāviṭa moḻik kūṟukaḷ cērkkappaṭa lāyiṉa. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar ēṟṟam (Dravidian ascent) paṟṟiya ik kōṭpāṭāṉatu tol māntar moḻiyiyal muṭivukaḷai mēlum cīrmai peṟac ceyya vallatu; nāḷaṭaivil 
mānta iṉat (AMH) tōṟṟamum paravalum; 
moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākkamum paravalum; 
varalāṟṟukku muntiya tolliyal 
ākiya mūṉṟu tuṟaikaḷilum aṉaittai yum viḷakkattakka oruṅkiṇainta oru peruṅ kōṭpāṭu (Grand synthesis) uruvākka vaḻi kōlak kūṭiyatu ākum.
21. tamatu IJDL cūṉ 2007 kaṭṭuraiyil sṭepāṉ leviṭ piṉvarumāṟu "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam koḷkaiyai ātarittuḷḷatu kuṟippiṭattakkatu.
"kaṭal maṭṭam ippoḻutuḷḷatai viṭa mikak kuṟaivāka irunta paṉiyūḻik kālattil āppirikkā - teṉṉintiyāvai iṇaitta vāl pōṉṟa nila iṇaippukaḷ / tīvukaḷ vaḻiyāka āp pirikkāviliruntu teṉ āciyāviṟku tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar vantirukkalāmeṉṉum kōṭ pāṭṭai pi. irāmanātaṉ valiyuṟuttukiṟār. ak kōṭpāṭṭai nāṉum ātarikkiṟēṉ. āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷaip paṟṟi āyvu ceyta aṟiñar ammakkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷ tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷuṭaṉ maṭṭumē toṭarpuḷḷavai eṉak kaṇ ṭuḷḷaṉar. uṟavumuṟai (Kinship) pūmarāṅ (vaḷaitaṭi ) payaṉpāṭu ākiyavaiyum ammakkaḷukkum tirā viṭarukkum uḷḷa toṭarpaik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. ki.mu. 6000ai oṭṭi ulakeṅkum kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu nilappakutikaḷiṉ parappu curuṅkiya poḻutu ulakiṉ piṟa pakuti makkaḷuṭaiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṭam iruntu tuṇṭikkappaṭṭaṉar āstirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷ. avarkaḷ teṉṉintiyāviliruntu āstirēliyāvukkup pulam peyarntatu ēṟattāḻa 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar irukkalām eṉkiṉṟa ṉar āstirēliya aṟiñar. itiliruntu kuṟaintatu 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarē tirāviṭar intiyā vil iruntaṉar eṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. inta ('tirāviṭar ēṟṟam") kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭu celkiṉṟaṉar; piṉṉar aṅkiruntu pāracīkat tiṟkum atait tāṇṭip piṟa nilappakutikaḷukkum celkiṉṟaṉar. antap piṟa nilappakutikaḷil mūla tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattiliruntu pirintu urā lik, al tāyik, intō - airōppiyam ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākiṉṟaṉa. intō - airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattukku tirāviṭam tāyā? tamakkaiyā? eṉpatai iṉṟaiya nilaiyil tiṭṭavaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātu. oppaṉ haimar (2003) The Real Eve : Modern Man Journey out of Africa nūliṉpaṭi marapaṇu āyvum (kākacas iṉattaic cuṭṭum M 17) tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar intiyāviliruntu vaṭakku vaṭamēṟkāka ēṟattāḻa 40000 āṇṭukku muṉṉar maiya kiḻakku - maiya āciyā - airōppā eṉṟavāṟu paṭip paṭiyākap paraviyirukkalām eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭiṟku etirāka illai eṉpataik kāṇalām (ṭākṭar leviṭ - takaval). 
ca. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paṇṭu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakkākap paraviya poḻutu (Dravidian Ascent) kuṭiyēṟiya pakutiyil uruvākiya cintuveḷi nākarikam.
22. cintuveḷi nākarikam 1947kku muntiya intiyā, pākistāṉ, vaṅkāḷa tēcam cērnta intiyāviṉ parappaḷavil kāl pakutikku mēl parappaḷavu koṇṭa peru nilap pakutiyil vaṭamēṟku intiyāvil paravi iruntatu. atāvatu cumār 5 laṭcam caturamail parappaḷavil vaḻaṅkiyatu. paṇṭaiya mecapotāmiya, ekiptiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ parappaiviṭa atika māṉa parappil cintuveḷi nākarikam vaḻaṅkiyatu. an nākarikam vaḻaṅkiya pakutiyil ki.mu. 2000 vākkil mottam aintiliruntu pattu laṭcam makkaḷ vāḻntirukkalām eṉpatu posel, par pōlā pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttu ākum (appoḻutu ulakil irunta motta makkaḷ tokaiyē 9 kōṭikkum kuṟaivu). cintu veḷi nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ 1500kkum mēṟpaṭṭa iṭaṅ kaḷil uḷḷaṉa. avaṟṟil cumār 20 iṭaṅkaḷil maṭṭumē - mokeñcōtarō, harappā uṭpaṭa - akaḻvāyvukaḷ naṭan tuḷḷaṉa. cintuveḷi nākarikat toṭakkam ki.mu. 7000 liruntu eṉpatai meharkār akaḻvāyvu niṟuviyuḷḷatu. an nāka rikattiṉ ciṟappuṟṟa nilai ki.mu. 3200 - 1800 kāla aḷavaic cārntatu. mokañcōtarō nakara makkaḷ tokai 40,000 eṉṟum harappā nakara makkaḷ tokai 25000 eṉṟum matip piṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa.
23. māntar iṉa (A MH) paraval, moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paṇṭu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakkākap paraviya poḻutu (Dravidian Ascent) kuṭiyēṟiya pakutiyē cintuveḷi nākarikap pakuti eṉṟu koḷvataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷai ituvarai kaṇ ṭōm. annākarikam tirāviṭac cārpuṭaiyatu eṉpataṟku uḷḷa mēlum pala valuvāṉa ātāraṅkaḷil cila varumāṟu:
1) mokañcōtārō - harappā iṭipāṭukaḷil tirāviṭak kaṭṭumāṉak kalaiyaik kāṇalām. vīṭukaḷil payaṉpaṭṭa poruḷkaḷ, nakai (āparaṇaṅkaḷ, parutti āṭaikaḷ aṉaittum tirāviṭac cārpuṭaiyavai. 
ii) tamiḻariṉ ciṟṟilakka muṟaiyaiyē cintuveḷi nākarika eṭai aḷavukaḷil kāṇkiṟōm eṉpataic ceṅkam ku. vēṅkaṭācalam niṟuviyuḷḷār.
iii) iṉṟaiya intu matattiṉ mutaṉmaik kūṟukaḷ aṉait tum tirāviṭa - tamiḻ - cintuveḷi nākarikak kūṟukaḷē ākum. camaṇa , - putta mataṅkaḷiṉ tōṟṟattukkum cintuveḷi maṟṟum vaṭanāṭṭil paṇṭu irunta tamiḻ (tirāviṭa) ñāṉikaḷē kāraṇamāvar. ikkaruttai ṭailar (1973) piṉvarumāṟu kūṟuvār;
iṉṟaiya vēta matak karuttukkaḷ - avaṟṟiṟku etirāṉa camaṇa puttam mutaliya karuttukkaḷ ivaṟ ṟiṟku iṭaiyē mutalil uṟaḻvu, piṉṉar iṇaippu eṉṟavāṟu intumatam uruvāṉatu eṉṟu karutuvatu avvaḷavu cariyāṉatalla. cintuveḷi nākarikam cārnta, paṇṭaiya tollintiya (tamiḻiya) nāka rikattiṉ puttuyirppē, maṟumalarcciyē iṉṟaiya intumatam ākum.
(The Hindu synthesis was less the dialectical reduction of orthodoxy and heterodoxy than the
resurgence of the ancient aboriginal Indus civilisation) maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷum, cīṉivāca aiyaṅkārum (1903,1923,1930) ikkaruttiṉarēyāvar.
iv) iṉṟaiya icaikkalai āriyar tamiḻariṭamiruntu kaṟṟatē (ō. kōsvāmi "intiya icai varalāṟu, 1957)
24. cintuveḷi akaḻvāyvukaḷukku neṭuṅkālam muṉṉarē heviṭ (1888). maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ (1903) pōṉṟa nuṇmāṇuḻai pulamikka aṟiñar āriyar varukaikku muṉṉar intiyā muḻuvatum vāḻntu vanta nākarika mikka tamiḻiya makkaḷiṭamiruntē āriyar nākarikam peṟṟirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟa karuttait teḷivākak kūṟiyiruntaṉar. ivaṟṟiṉ virivai pi. irāmanātaṉ : cintu veḷit tol tamiḻ nākarikam 1999 iyalkaḷ 3-6 maṟṟum 8 il kāṇka).
25. speyiṉ nāṭṭaic cārnta tirāviṭar eṉṟu taṉṉai aḻaittuk koṇṭa es. hīrās pātiriyār (1191888 - 14.12. 1955) toṉmai intō - naṇṇilakkarai nākarika āyvu (Studies in Proto - Indo Mediterranean Culture 1953) eṉṉum nūlil cintu veḷi nākarikam tirāviṭaruṭaiyatu; avarkaḷ moḻi tirāviṭa moḻi eṉpatai niṟuviyuḷḷār. mikap paḻaṅ kālattil (ki.mu. 5000kkum neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉar) mutaṟkaṇ kāvirik karaiyil uruvākiya arappā nākarikam intiya mēṟkukkarai vaḻiyākat tamiḻarkaḷāl cintuveḷi, cumēriyā, ekiptu naṇṇilakkarai nāṭukaḷ varai koṇṭu cellappaṭṭatu eṉpatu hīrās koḷkai (avar vātat tukku ātāramākak koṇṭa ceytikaḷ cila ippoḻutu tavaṟākat tōṉṟalām; cintuveḷi muttiraikaḷai avar tirāviṭa moḻi cārntavai eṉṟu koṇṭa muṭivu cari yeṉṟālum avar muttiraikaḷil paṭittuk kaṇṭa vācakaṅkaḷ iṉṟu ēṟkattakkaṉavāka illai. iruntālum avar kaṇṭa aṭippaṭai uṇmai iṉṟum valuvuṭaiyatākavē uḷḷatu.)
26. eṉ lākōvari 1963il veḷiyiṭṭa tirāviṭar tōṟṟamum mēl nāṭukaḷum (Dravidian Origins and the West) eṉṉum nūl ciṟanta moḻiyiyal meymmaikaḷai uṇarttu vatākum. 'tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkarai nāṭukaḷiliruntu pulam peyarntu vantu intiyāvil cintuveḷiyil mutaṟkaṇ kuṭiyēṟip piṉṉar kumari varai paraviṉar" eṉṟa apattakkoḷ kaiyai avar (vivaram puriyāmal) piṉpaṟṟiyirunta pōtilum avar niṟuviya piṉvarum meymmaikaḷ mukkiyamāṉavai :
i) 6000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar intiyāviliruntu aṭlāṇṭik kaṭalvarai orē mātiriyāṉa "pala col piṇippu oṭṭunilai' (Polysynthetic Suffixal) moḻikaḷ iṭaiyīṭu iṉṟip paraviyiruntaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ coṟ kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ ōraḷavukku oṉṟupōl iruntaṉa. potuc col viḻukkāṭu moḻikku moḻi māṟupaṭṭu iruntirukkalām. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, elāmaiṭ, cumēriyaṉ, harri, kākēciyaṉ, halṭi , pōṉṟavai orē moḻiyamaippuk koṇṭu oṉṟukkoṉṟu uṟavuṭaiyavaiyākac caṅkilit toṭarpōla amaintiruntaṉa.
ii) intō airōppiya moḻi pēcuvōr (ki.mu 2000ai oṭṭi airōppāvukkuḷ kiḻakkiliruntu nuḻaiyumuṉṉar aṅku pēcappaṭṭu vanta moḻikaḷil oṉṟāṉa pāsku (yūskarā) iṉṟum pirāṉcukkum speyiṉukkum iṭaippaṭṭa pireṉīs malaippakutiyil pēcappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi inta pāsku moḻiyuṭaṉ maṭṭumaṉṟi intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukku muṉṉar airōppāvil pēcappaṭṭa toṉmoḻikaḷuṭaṉum uṟavuṭaiyatu.
iii) immoḻikaḷ aṉaittum oṉṟukkoṉṟu neruṅ kiya toṭarpuḷḷavai; avai potuvāṉa orē tāy moḻi yiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷ eṉṟē kūṟalām.
27. cintuveḷi nākarikam intō āriya moḻi pēcunar uruvākkiyatāka irukkalām eṉa iṉṟaiya intiya aṟiñar (kuṟippāka vaṭanāṭṭaṟiñar) cilar kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. itu tavaṟu eṉpataic caṟṟē viḷakkuvōm. vēta moḻiyākiya intō airōppiya moḻi intiyāvil uruvākiyirukkalām eṉṟu vēta camaskirutappaṟṟāḷar kūṟuvatu apattam (itu vēṟu; ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉar tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiliruntu kiḷaitta moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷuḷ intō airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpamum oṉṟu eṉṟa ñāṉappirakācar - tēvanēyaṉ kōṭpāṭu vēṟu!) ki.mu. 1700 -1500 kālakaṭṭattil intiyā viṟkuḷ vaṭamēṟkiliruntu nuḻaintavarkaḷ vētamoḻi (Vedic Language) ākiya intō āriya moḻi pēcunar āvar. ivar kaḷ cintuveḷi nākarika makkaḷōṭu oppiṭum pōtu ciṟu eṇṇikkaiyiṉarākavē iruntirukka vēṇṭum. intō airōppiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiyavai intō āriya moḻiyākiya vētamoḻi tavira irāṉiyaṉ (avestaṉ), aṉaṭōliyaṉ (hiṭṭaiṭ), armīṉiyaṉ, tōkkāriyaṉ, alpēṉiyaṉ, kirīkku , ittālik (ilattīṉ mutaliyavai), kelṭik (airiṣ, velṣ uṭpaṭa), jermāṉik (āṅkilam uṭpaṭa), pālṭik (laṭviyaṉ, littuvēṉiyaṉ), slāv (raṣyaṉ mutaliyavai) ākiyavaiyākum. intap pal vēṟu intō airōppiya moḻi pēcunarum oruṅku cērntu ki.mu. 4000 - 3000 kāla aḷavil karuṅkaṭal - kāspiyaṉ kaṭal pakutikku vaṭakkil mattiya āciya - airōppiya sṭeppi pulveḷikaḷil vacittu vanta nāṭōṭikaḷ (nomads) āvar. ak kālakkaṭṭattil avarkaḷ intō airōppiyat toṉ moḻiyaip pēcivantaṉar. ki.mu. 3000 - 2000 kāla aḷavil teṉ kiḻakkākavum nakaralāyiṉar. mēṟkil ceṉṟa nāṭōṭik kuḻuviṉaruḷ oṉṟāṉa kirīkku moḻi pēcunar nuḻainta pakuti yil (tirāviṭac cārpāṉa) appakutip paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷuṭaṉ kalantu kirīkku moḻi pēcunar uṭaṉaṭiyāka nākarikam peṟṟaṉar. kiḻakku teṉkiḻakkākap peyarnta nāṭōṭik kuḻuviṉaruḷ intō āriya moḻi pēcum kuḻuvum oṉṟu. ki.mu. 1700 - 1500il avarkaḷ intiyāvukkuḷ irāṉiliruntu nuḻainta poḻutu avarkaḷum cintuveḷit tol nākarikattiṉariṭam miruntu viraivil nākarikam peṟṟaṉar. cintuveḷit tirāviṭa aṟiñarkaḷ (avarkaḷil cilar riṣikaḷākavum ēṟkappaṭṭaṉar) paṭaippukaḷum nēraṭiyākavō moḻi peyarkkappaṭṭō rik vētattilēyē ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ (1903, 1923, 1930), vālparṭ pōṉṟōr karuttākum.
taṅka ḷuṭaiya The Rise of Civilisation in India and Pakistan (1988nūlil piriṭjaṭ maṟṟum rēmāṇṭ ālciṉ tampatiyar. karuttum ituvē :
(The earliest Indo-Aryan text, the compiled Rg Veda, shoWS several influences of a non-Indo-Aryan, Dravidian element, in the form of phonetic changes, introduction of loan words and names etc.These presuppose the coexistence of the Vedic and Dravidian speaking peoples in a cultural contact situation for a period, perhaps of centuries, before the compilation of the Rg Veda (circa 15001300BC)
28. mutaṟkaṇ intō āriya moḻi pēcunarukkum cintu veḷi nākarikat tamiḻarukkum (tirāviṭarukkum iṭaiyē vaṉ muṟai ēṟpaṭṭa pōtilum viraivil iruvakaiyārum kalantu viṭṭaṉar. āriyarāl nakaraṅkaḷ tākkappaṭṭirukkalām. intira ṉiṉ peyarē purantaraṉ (nakaraṅkaḷai aḻippavaṉ) tāṉē nakaraṅ kaḷiṉ vīḻccikku ki.mu. 1700ai oṭṭi nikaḻnta iyaṟkai, cuṟṟup puṟac cūḻnilaik kāraṇaṅkaḷum ōraḷavu kāraṇamāyirun tirukkalām eṉṟu ālciṉ (1995) kūṟukiṟār. āṉāl cintu veḷi nākarikam paraviyirunta ciṟṟūrppuṟap perunilap parappil (ciṟu eṇṇikkaiyil nuḻainta) āriyarāl peruntāk kam ēṟpaṭuttiyirukka muṭiyātu. ākavētāṉ cunītikumār cāṭṭarji, vālṭar pēr carvīs, es.ē. ṭailar pōṉṟōr kūṟuvatu pōla iṉṟaiya nākarikam, paṇpāṭu, intumatam ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭittaḷam (rūpāykku 12 aṇā aḷavukku eṉpār caṭṭarji) tirāviṭa (tamiḻ) moḻi, paṇpāṭu āki yavaiyē. intō āriya moḻi pēcunar paraval paṟṟiya varalāṟṟiliruntē avarkaḷ ki.mu. 1700kku muntiya cintu nākarikattai uruvākkiyirukka iyalātu eṉpatu teriyum. cintu veḷi nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷil āriyarukku nerukkamāṉa kutirai kiṭaiyātu eṉpatum mukkiyamāṉatu.
29. intō āriya moḻiyiṉ rikvēta nilaiyilēyē nuḻaintuḷḷa tirāviṭac coṟkaḷāka cumār nāṟpatu - aimpatu coṟkaḷai parō, emaṉō pōṉṟavarkaḷ ēṟkeṉavē aṭaiyāḷam kaṇṭuḷḷaṉar. tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟi mukam (1990) nūlil itaik kuṟippiṭum cuvelapil, rik vētak kālattiliruntē intō āriya moḻi kaṭaṉ peṟṟuḷḷa ēṟattāḻa 10 moḻiyiyaṟ kūṟukaḷaiyum paṭṭiyaliṭṭuḷḷār. (ivaiyellām vēta camaṟkiruta moḻiyil tirāviṭa moḻi yiṉ tākkam eṉṟa aḷaviltāṉ ivarkaḷ āyvukaḷ uḷḷaṉa. āṉāl ñāṉap pirakācarum pāvāṇarum kaṇṭa muṭivu kaḷ ivaṟṟaiviṭa mika viñciyavai. avviruvarum "intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ aṭippaṭai vērc coṟkaḷ palavum tol - tirāviṭa moḻiyiliruntu peṟṟavaiyē" eṉṟa karuttuṭaiyavar.
30. cintuveḷi nākarikam tirāviṭa nākarikam eṉṟum anta nākarika muttirai eḻuttukkaḷ tol tamiḻē eṉṟum takuti vāynta aṟiñar aṉaivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. tamatu Indus Script Dravidian (1995) nūlil irā. mativāṇaṉ, cintu veḷiyil muntu tamiḻ (2004) nūlil pūrṇa cantira jīvā ākiyōr av veḻuttukkaḷait toltamiḻākap paṭikkum poḻutu tol kāppiyattiliruntu kiṭṭum moḻiyiyal taravukaḷaiyum payaṉpaṭuttiyuḷḷaṉar. puṟanāṉūṟu 201 202 il kuṟippiṭappaṭum iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr cintuveḷi nākarikattōṭu toṭarpuṭaiyavarkaḷākak kapilarāl karutappaṭṭaṉar. puṟanāṉūṟu 202 kuṟippiṭum araiyam rikvētam 6.27.5 il cuṭṭappaṭum hariyūpiyā tāṉ eṉpatu pi.el. cāmi karuttu (centamiḻc celvi caṉavari 1994). araiyam = aracamaram. araiya + akappā = araiyakappā = harappā eṉṟu peyar māṟiyatu eṉkiṟār avar. iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr 49 talaimuṟaikaḷukku muṉṉar (atāvatu tol paḻaṅkālattil) pukaḻpeṟṟa tuvarai eṉṉum kōṭṭai nakarai āṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟu puṟam 201 kūṟukiṟatu. tamatu tolkāppiyap pāyira vuraiyil nacciṉārkkiṉiyar vēḷir tuvārapati (tuvārakaiyil liruntu vantavarkaḷ eṉkiṉṟār. araiyam harappāvait tāṉ kuṟippatākak koṇṭāl harappā paṟṟiyum tuvārakai paṟṟiyum kapilar kālattil vaḻaṅkiya (oru nakarukkuriyatai maṟṟataṟkuriyatāka māṟṟi vaḻaṅkiya) toṉmak karuttaip puṟam 201,202 pāṭalkaḷ kūṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉka. itaṉai jīvā mēlum āyvu ceykiṟār. vēḷ - vēṭ - pēṭ (Bet) - pēṭ tuvārakā eṉṟu tuvārakaiyiṉ peyar varalāṟṟai avar tarukiṟār.
31. cumēriya nākarika muttiraikaḷil ciṅkaṅkaḷ iraṇṭaik kollum kilkāmeṣ uruvam kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. kilkāmēṣ paṟṟiya toṉmak kataiyum cumēriyap poṟippukaḷil tarap paṭukiṟatu. cintuveḷi muttiraikaḷ iraṇṭil (kilkāmēṣ ciṅkaṅkaḷait tākkuvatu pōlavē) intiya vīraṉ oruvaṉ pulikaḷ iraṇṭai kaikku oṉṟākak kolvatu pōl cit tirikkappaṭṭuḷḷāṉ. puṟam 201 iruṅkōvēḷaip 'puli kaṭimāl' eṉṟu aḻaikkiṟatu. iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr kālam cintuveḷi nākarikak kālattiliruntu toṭaṅki yirukkalām eṉa ai.makātēvaṉ Journal of Tamil Studies mē 1970 itaḻil uṉṉittiruntār. āyiṉum 2002 caṉavariyil avar karuttu anta uṉṉippiṟku valuvāṉa ātāram illai eṉpatākum.
32. rikvētam ai . 133,1 & 3iṉ tamiḻ vaṭivam varumāṟu; 
"ō makavāṉ aḻintupaṭṭa vailastāṉaka nakarattilum, aḻintupaṭṭa makāvailasta nakarattilum uḷḷa cūṉiyak kārikaḷ kumpalkaḷai aḻittu oḻippāyāka" 
"nāṉ mēlulakattaiyum pūmiyaiyum cattiyattiṉāl tūymaip paṭuttukiṟēṉ. vailastāṉa nakaril intiraṉāl tōṟkaṭikkap paṭṭu kolaiyuṇṭu kiṭakkum intiraṉai eritta āṟṟal mikka tuṣṭap picācukaḷai nāṉ erittu oḻikkiṟēṉ" 
āriyarkaḷāl nācamākkappaṭṭa nakarattiṉ peyarāṉa 'vailas tāṉam' eṉpatu āriya moḻiyallāta piṟamoḻic collāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu ṭākṭar parō karuttu ākum. (jarṉal āp intiyaṉ hisṭari XII -1 ēpral 1963) orukkāl 'vailastāṉam' eṉpatu vēḷiruṭaiya ūrnakaraik kuṟittirukkalām.
33. cintu mutaliya āṟukaḷil cintuveḷit tirāviṭarkaḷ kaṭṭiyirunta aṇaikaḷai āriyar uṭaittu nāṭṭai veḷḷak kāṭākki aḻittirukkalām eṉpar tāmōtar tarmāṉant kōcampi (The culture and Civilisation of Ancient India in historical outline 1974). maturaik kāñci 725 ām aṭiyilum aka nāṉūṟu 346 ām pāṭalilum āṟṟiṉ kuṟukkē kaṭṭappaṭum aṇaikaḷaik kuṟikka 'kaṟciṟai' eṉṉum col payaṉpaṭukiṟatu. rik vētattil aṇaiyaik kuṟippiṭum cirā (Sira) eṉṉum col 'kaṟciṟaiyiṉ' citaivē eṉpar pi.el. cāmi (centamiḻc celvi : 1994 navampar)
ru. cintuveḷi nākarika muttirai eḻuttukkaḷ tamiḻiya moḻiyākap paṭikkattakkavaiyē eṉpatum tamiḻ vari vaṭivam cintu veḷi varivaṭivattiliruntē uruvāki yirukkalām eṉpatum
34. cintuveḷi nākarika muttirai eḻuttukkaḷ eṉpavai mūvāyirattukku mēṟpaṭṭa "sṭīyaṭṭaiṭ" (cavarkkārakkal, atāvatu mākkal ) muttirai kaḷilum cila cepput takaṭukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟilum uḷḷavai. ovvoru muttiraiyum cumār 20 mi.mī. x 30 mi.mī. aḷavuḷḷatu. cila caturamāṉavai. perum pālaṉavaṟṟil oru vilaṅkiṉ uruvamum ataṉ mēl pakkattil oṉṟiliruntu pattu paṉṉiraṇṭu (carācari 5) kuṟiyīṭukaḷum uḷḷaṉa. mika nīṇṭa toṭar 26 kuṟiyīṭukaḷ koṇṭatu. cintu veḷiyiliruntu paruttit tuṇi pōṉṟavaṟṟai cumēriyā pōṉṟa mēlai nāṭukaḷukku ēṟṟumati ceyyumpoḻutu cippaṅkaḷ kaṭṭi avaṟṟiṉ mēl poruḷukkuriyavar peyaraik kaḷimaṇṇil muttiraiyiṭa im muttiraikaḷuḷ perum pālāṉavai payaṉpaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatē ciṟanta āyvaṟiñar kaḷ karuttu. vīlar, haṇṭa r, kāṭ (Gadd), kōcampi, kō (Coe) ākiyōr inta aṭippaṭaiyiltāṉ muttiraik kuṟiyīṭukaḷil uḷḷavai taṉi āṭkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷākattāṉ (cila nērvu kaḷil paṭṭaṅkaḷuṭaṉ) irukka vēṇṭum eṉṟa muṭivukku vantuḷḷaṉar. cippattaik kaṭṭiya kayiṟu, pāy ivaṟṟiṉ cuvaṭukaḷ cila kaḷimaṇ kaṭṭikaḷil kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. (cila kaṭṭikaḷ orōvaḻi neruppiṉ vāyppaṭṭu cuṭappaṭṭatāl avai maṭṭumē iṉṟu kiṭaikkiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiltāṉ iv vaṭaiyāḷaṅkaḷ terikiṉṟaṉa) āka, ālciṉ (1998) kūṟuvatu pōl "im muttiraikaḷiṉ payaṉpāṭu (allatu payaṉpāṭu kaḷil oṉṟu) vāṇika naṭavaṭikkaikaḷ cārntatāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatil aiyamillai " (There can be little doubt that the Harappan seals were - atleast as one of their functions - necessary elements in the mechanism of trade. p. 185.) cila muttiraikaḷ tāyat tukkaḷākavum nērttik kaṭaṉ villaikaḷākavum aṭaiyāḷa ilacciṉaikaḷākavum payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭu irukkalām.
35. pala muttiraikaḷil oṟṟaik kompu teriyum erutu oṉṟiṉ muṉṉar eṉṟa vaṭivam uḷḷatu. makātēvaṉ karuttu (1985, 1984) ivvaṭivam cōmaccāṟu vaṭikkappaṭṭa ēṉam; piṉṉar cōmaccāṟu āriyarkaḷālum mukkiyamāṉatāka ēṟkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum. piṟkālattil ivvaṭivam intirat vajam āṉatu. aḷakkuṭi cīttārāmaṉ karūril kaṇṭeṭutta ceppu muttiraiyilum inta vaṭivam uḷḷatu eṉpatu makā tēvaṉ karuttu ākum.
36. cintuveḷi eḻuttuk kuṟiyīṭukaḷ ēṟattāḻa 400 aḷavil uḷḷaṉa. (parpōlā 385, makātēvaṉ 417). 2906 muttirai vācakaṅkaḷil kaṇṭa kuṟiyīṭukaḷai makātēvaṉ piṉvarumāṟu vakaippaṭuttukiṟār:

80 viḻukkāṭu taṭavaikku mēl varum kuṟiyīṭukaḷ 67 maṭṭumē yākum. (pātikku mēṟpaṭṭa kuṟiyīṭukaḷ ovvoṉṟum pattu taṭavaikkum kuṟaivā kavē varukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟuḷḷum 112 ovvoṉṟum oru taṭavaiyē varukiṟatu). āka, inta 67 kuṟiyīṭukaḷukkum mutaṉmai tantu vācikka muṟpaṭuvatu nallatu eṉkiṟār makātēvaṉ. mottam cumār 400 kuṟiyīṭukaḷil aṭippaṭaik kuṟiyīṭukaḷ 200 eṉpatu parpōlā karuttu.
37. cintuveḷi muttirai eḻuttukkaḷ paḻantamiḻē (paḻan tirāviṭamē) eṉpatai ittuṟai vallunar aṉaivarum ēṟ kiṉṟaṉar. ivaṟṟaip paṭittuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷ (parpōlā, makā tēvaṉ, mativāṇaṉ, pūrṇa cantira jīvā , pēr carvīs mutali yōr) ovvoruvarum ovvoru vakaiyāka vācit tuḷḷaṉar. muttiraiyaik kuttiyapiṉ kiṭaikkum muttiraip pativil uḷḷa vācakaṅkaḷai (Impressions) valamiruntu iṭamākavē perumpālōr paṭikkiṉṟaṉar. (mativāṇaṉ maṭṭum iṭamiruntu valamākap paṭittuḷḷār. eṉiṉum im muttirai eḻuttukkaḷ, tirāviṭa moḻiyaic cārntatākat tāṉ irukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu paṟṟi maṭṭum aṟiñarkaḷiṭam orumitta karuttu uḷḷatu; piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟaik kāṇka :
sṭāṉli vārparṭ : intiyāvukku ōr aṟimukam eṉṟa nūlil uḷḷa "pala vakaiyāṉa ātāraṅkaḷiliruntum nām karutuvatu avarkaḷ tol tirāviṭarkaḷ eṉpatum, tamiḻukkup pāṭṭaṉ muṟaiyākak kūṭiya oru moḻi avarkaḷ moḻiyāka iruntirukkalām eṉpatum ākum."
“We assume from various shreds of evidence that they are were proto Dravidian, possibly using a language that was a grandfather of modern Tamil” (Stanley Wolpert: An Introduction to India, University of California Press 1991)
jē.em. rāparṭs : peṅkuyiṉ ulaka varalāṟu "teṉ ṉintiyāvil iṉṟum vaḻaṅkivarum tirāviṭa moḻikaḷōṭu iyaipuṭaiya oru moḻiyaic cārntavaiyāka (cintuveḷi muttiraic coṟkaḷ) irukkalāmeṉat tōṉṟukiṟatu."
It now seems at least likely that they are part of a language akin to the Dravidian tongues still used in southern India (J.M. Roberts History of the World Pelican 1992).
kamil cuvelapil : tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟimukam ''cintu veḷi eḻuttukkaḷiṉ moḻi eṉṉa eṉṟu iṟutiyāka niṟuvappaṭum poḻutu atu tirāviṭa moḻi cārntatāka amaivataṟkē vāyppu mika atikam.''
The most probable candidate is and remains some form of Dravidian” (Dravidian Liguistics - An Introduction. Pondicherry 1990; Chap VI : Dravidian and Harapan)
38. cintu veḷi muttirai eḻuttukkaḷiliruntē caṅka kālat tamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatu mativāṇaṉ karuttu. ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 700kku muṉṉamē tamiḻukku ōr eḻuttu vari vaṭivam iruntatu; ataip piṉpaṟṟiyē acōka pirāmi eḻuttu uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉak koḷvataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ uṇṭu. 2006 pipravari mātattil mayilāṭutuṟai - cem piyaṉ kaṇṭiyūril vi. caṇmukanātaṉ kaṇṭeṭutta putuk kaṟkālak karuvi oṉṟil cintuveḷi eḻuttukkaḷ nāṉku uḷḷaṉa. avaṟṟai ai. makātēvaṉ "muruku aṉ" (murukaṉ) eṉṟu paṭittuḷḷār. akkaruviyiṉ kālam ki.mu. 2000 -1500 āka irukkalām eṉṟu avar karutukiṟār. putuk kaṟkālat tamiḻ makkaḷum akkālattil vāḻnta cintuveḷi nākarika makkaḷum  orē moḻiyaiyē pēciṉār eṉṟum ammoḻi tirāviṭa moḻiyē eṉṟum ik kaṇṭupiṭippu niṟuvukiṟatu eṉpatum avar karuttu . ("The Neolithic people of Tamilnadu and the Indus Valley people shared the same language which can only be Dravidian and not Indo-Aryan"); 152006. intu nāḷitaḻ.
cu. varalāṟṟuk kālattukku caṅka kālattukku) muntaiya (Proto Historic) kālat tamiḻaka varalāṟu ki.mu. 1000 - ki.mu. 300) 
39. tamiḻaka varalāṟṟil varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭakkam eṉa potuvāka iṉṟu ēṟkappaṭum caṅka kālam kimu. 300 - ki.pi. 300 cārntatu. ataṟku muntiya varalāṟṟukku muntiya kālam" ākiya ki.mu. 1000 - 300 paṟṟiya ceytikaḷait tiṟampaṭat taruvatu rājaṉ (2004) tolliyal nōkkil caṅkakālam ākum. āticcanallūril uḷḷa ki.mu. 1000 kālattiya iṭukāṭu 114 ēkkar parappuḷḷatu. atil 1 viḻukkāṭu maṭṭumē akaḻvāyvu ceyyappaṭṭuḷḷatu. koṭumaṇalil (caṅkakālak koṭu maṇam) 50 ēkkar parappuḷḷa paḻaiya vāḻviṭattilum 1 viḻukkāṭu maṭṭumē akaḻvāyvu ceyyappaṭṭuḷḷatu. inta oru viḻukkāṭu akaḻvāyvukaḷil kiṭaitta eccaṅkaḷiliruntē kimu 1000 mutal tamiḻakattil vāḻnta makkaḷē, iṭaiyīṭiṉṟi, caṅka ilakkiyam vivarikkum tamiḻar camutāya makkaḷāyiṉar eṉṟu niṟuva iyaṉṟuḷḷa tu. makātēvaṉ (2003) Early Tamil Epigraphy nūlil tamiḻik kalveṭṭukaḷ evaṟṟaiyum ki.mu. 200kku muntiyatākak karutavillai. āṉāl avaṟṟai (kuṟippāka koṭumaṇal pāṉai ōṭṭut tamiḻip poṟippukaḷai) ki.mu 4 allatu 5 nūṟṟāṇṭiṉavākat tāṉ koḷḷa vēṇṭum . eṉpataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷai rājaṉ tantuḷḷār. mēlē pakuti āṟaiyum kīḻē patti 42 aiyum kāṇka.
40. kimu 1000 - kimu. 300 kālakaṭṭamē tamiḻakattiṉ irumpukkālam allatu peruṅkaṟpaṭaic ciṉṉak (Megalithic) kālam eṉṟu kuṟippiṭap paṭukiṟatu. akkāla kaṭṭattil iṟanta varkaḷ tāḻiyil putaikkap paṭṭaṉar. kaṟpatukkaikaḷil aṭakkam ceyyappaṭṭaṉar. peruṅkaṟpaṭaic ciṉṉaṅkaḷ cila viṭaṅkaḷil iruntaṉa; cila viṭaṅkaḷil illai; eṉiṉum ikkālam "peruṅkaṟpaṭaic ciṉṉakkālam" eṉṟē potuvāka kuṟippiṭappaṭukiṟatu.
41. ki.mu. 1000 aḷavil makkaḷ perumaḷavukku āṭumāṭu vaḷarppavarkaḷākavē iruntaṉar. vēḷāṇmai toṭakka nilaiyil iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu akaḻvāyvukaḷiṉ muṭivākum. koṭumaṇal, tāṇṭikkuṭi pāṉai ōṭṭut tamiḻiṉ poṟippuk kālam ki.mu. 5 allatu 4 ām nūṟṟāṇṭākattāṉ irukka vēṇṭum eṉa rājaṉ niṟuviyuḷḷār. appoḻutē eḻuttaṟivu peṟṟa camutāyam tamiḻakattil iruntatu. eṉavē vara lāṟṟuk kālamē tamiḻakattil ki.mu. 500liruntu toṭaṅki yatākak koḷḷa iṭamuṇṭu eṉpār rājaṉ.
42. tamiḻiya moḻi pēcunar kimu 2000kku muṉṉar iruntē tamiḻakattilum teṉṉāṭṭilum iṭaiviṭātu vāḻntu vanta maṇṇiṉ maintarē eṉpataiyē tolliyal āyvukaḷ kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatai ālciṉ tampatiyar 1988 tam nūlil piṉvarumāṟu kūṟuvar.
"teṉṉintiyā iṉṟu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ vaḻaṅkum pakuti. am moḻikaḷaip pēcuvōr pūrvīkam eṉṉavāka iruntirukkalām? avarkaḷ (vaṭamēṟkiliruntu) teṉṉintiyā viṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟumpōtu tammuṭaṉ irumpaiyum peruṅ kaṟpaṭai nākarikattaiyum koṇarntaṉar eṉa (valuvāṉa ātāramiṉṟik kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. tolliyal cāṉṟukaḷiṉpaṭi ippaṭi naṭantirukka vāyppu mikak kuṟaivu. irumpup payaṉ pāṭṭiṟku 1000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē karunāṭakat til (teṉ intiyāvil piṟa pakutikaḷilum kūṭa irukkalām). peruṅkaṟpaṭai nākarikak kuṭiyiruppukaḷ iruntuḷḷaṉa; avai iṭaiyīṭu iṉṟi toṭarntu iruntu vantuḷḷaṉa ; viyakkat takka paṇpāṭṭut toṭarcciyuṭaṉ iṉṟum uḷḷa paṇ pāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷ pala antap paḻaiya nākarikak kūṟukaḷiṉ toṭarcciyākavē tōṉṟu kiṉṟaṉa. uṭaṟkūṟu amaippilum antap paḻaiya nākarika makkaḷum iṉṟaiya makkaḷum perum aḷavukku oṉṟu pōlavē uḷḷaṉar. eṉavē teṉ ṉintiyāvil (pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē ) putukkaṟkāla nākarikam paṭaitta makkaḷiṭaiyē uruvāṉavaiyē tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ eṉpatai maṟuppatu kaṭiṉamāka uḷḷatu. teṉṉintiyāvil paṟpala pakutikaḷil irunta putukkaṟkāla nākarika makkaḷiṭaiyē toṭarpu iruntirukkum; potuvāṉa tirāviṭa moḻiyum iruntirukkum. ki.mu. 3000 - 1000 kāla aḷavil teṉṉintiyāvil irunta putuk kaṟkālak kuṭiyiruppu kaḷil iruntu piṟantavaiyē varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭak kattaic cārnta kuṭiyiruppukaḷum eṉalām.''
The southern part of the peninsula is today the homeland of the Dravidian language, and we may well enquire what - speaking in broadest terms - is likely to have been their history. It has been claimed, though not on very solid grounds, that the earliest speakers of these languages brought with them into Peninsular India both iron and the custom of making megalithic graves. In the light of archaeological evidence this appears to be extremely improbable. We now know that at least for a millennium prior to the arrival of iron, there were established settlements in Karnataka, and probably also in other parts of the peninsula, and these settlements show evidence of a remarkable continuity of culture. Many modern culture traits appear to derive from them, and a substantial part of the populations shows physical affiliation to the Neolithic people. In the light of all this it is difficult to believe that the Dravidian languages do not owe their origin to the same people who produced the Neolithic culture there. ..... the several regional cultures .... had throughout a degree of interaction and probably originally a common language family, Dravidian. ..... the settlements of the third - second millennia appear to be ancestral to those which we encounter there from the beginnings of history onwards. (p. 353) 
peruṅkaṟpaṭai nākarikam tamiḻnāṭṭiliruntē vaṭakkē takkāṇattiṟkup paraviyatu eṉṟu tolliyalaṟiñar ē. cattiyamūrtti 26.5.2007 avarkaḷum intu nāḷitaḻil terivittuḷḷār.

4. pāvāṇar tamatu vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1967) nūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 63 - 269l "vaṭamoḻip pukunta teṉ coṟkaḷ" mēlum ērāḷamāṉavaṟṟiṉ paṭṭiyalait tantuḷḷār – parō emeṉō pōṉṟavar ēṟkeṉavē kaṇṭa coṟkaḷ cilavum avaṟṟukkum mēlāka pāvāṇar kaṇṭa coṟkaḷ palavumāka) teṉcol mūlat tiricoṟkaḷ, teṉ collaṭip puṇarppuc coṟkaḷ, tamiḻiliruntu moḻipeyarttuk koṇṭa coṟkaḷ, irupiṟappikaḷ (hybrids); vaṭavar pōliyāṉa cams kiruta vēr kāṭṭum coṟkaḷ ; palvakait tirippukaḷāl ''vaṭacoṟkaḷ'' eṉac cātitta coṟkaḷ; tamiḻc cuṭṭuc coṟkaḷ / viṉāc coṟkaḷaip piṉpaṟṟi vaṭamoḻiyil uruvāṉa coṟkaḷ ; tamiḻaip piṉpaṟṟi vaṭamoḻiyil uruvākkiya puṇarcciyilakkaṇam mutaliyavai ākiyavai paṟṟi annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 269 - 321 l kāṇalām.
4. tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu vētamoḻi, camskirutam vaṭa intiya (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattiṉavākak karutappaṭum) moḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟil ēṟiyuḷḷa oliyaṉiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal kūṟukaḷ palavaṟṟai emaṉō tamatu 1956 kaṭṭuraiyil niṟuviṉār.
(1) oliyaṉiyal
ṭa; ḷa (rikvētattil), ṇa ākiya vaḷaināvolikaḷ (retroflex/ cerebral/domal tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟavai. avvolikaḷaik koṇṭa coṟkaḷait tamiḻil liruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟa piṉ olikaḷaiyum vaṇṇamālaiyil cērtta ṉar.
(ii) viṉaiyeccam (gerund/absolutive/incomplete verb/ Past nonfinite verb/ converb / indeclinable Participle/ adverbial Participle/ Past participle (Jothimuthu) / conjunctive Participle (Grierson) rik vēta kālattilēyē ēṟiviṭṭatu.
(iii) nēr kūṟṟu muṭintavuṭaṉ " eṉa/ eṉṟu tamiḻil varuvatu pōl ("taṉ ceyviṉai muṭitteṉak kēṭpal - puṟam 27:9-10) camaskirutattil kūṟṟukkup piṉṉar "iti" iti (= ippaṭik kūṟiṉāṉ) eṉa varutal
(iv) etirolic coṟkaḷ (echo words) - "pulikili'' pōṉṟavai
(v) olikkuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - caḷacaḷ, paṭapaṭa pōṉṟavai
(vi) ōṭu, ōṭṭu, ōṭṭuvi; naṭa, naṭattu, naṭattuvi pōṉṟa, vāypāṭṭu (simplex, eausative, causative of causative) viṉai vaṭivaṅkaḷ (vii) emeṉō 1974 kaṭṭurai : tamiḻ "um" maip payaṉ pāṭukaḷaip pōṉṟa camaskiruta api api ai payaṉpaṭuttutal
(viii) mēlatu kaṭṭurai: tirāviṭa uṟavu muṟaiccoṟ kaḷilum cari intō āriya uṟavu muṟaic coṟkaḷilum perumpālum toḻilaik kuṟikkum cātip peyar āṇukku uḷḷatu; āṉāl peṇṇaik kuṟikkum poḻutu intac cātikkāraṉuṭaiya makaḷ maṉaivi tāy/piṟa uṟavu eṉṟa aḷavilēyē amaintu viṭukiṟatu.
5. muṉ pattiyiṟkaṇṭa ceytikaḷai kāliṉ mācikā tamatu Defining a Linguistic Area: South Asia (1976) nūlil virivāka viḷakkiyuḷḷār. tamiḻiya moḻikaḷai yoṭṭiya māṟṟaṅ kaḷē intō āriya moḻikaḷil ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu avar karuttu .' (India) is a fairly stable typological area where the brunt of the burden of adaptation is borne by intrusive rather than local languages"
